The boy filed that third movement on a place of its own beside the Day 161 fraction at the damper and the Day 166 held beat beneath the shelf and forbade the three to stand up as a rule. The crust stayed stiff two inches from his ear with its empty place unnamed, the two rooms did not touch, and the costs of the week went to pots, weather and rivers without a figure. *He's not Vail* stays sealed, the foundling's name ungiven, no bond paid and no payer named.

**Ch 42 (bonds).** Nothing changed hands across the firing month's last seven days. Sella still carried him at the half-pace, gave every instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom and the running water and never to the basket, said the house, kept the widow's mark untouched at every shelf, well, rack, road, basket, market, post and dusk, and on Day 179 set him in the moot-post's shade where no slate lay without a word to the basket about the turn. Tarn kept six counted and the seventh held, nine stoppings with no tenth and the loan uncalled; Jorra kept his dust, his withheld note and his waiting finger, *he's not Vail* sealed; Berr went off with the box locked and his good reason intact; Hessa carried the slate unread to Greybank. No name given, no bond paid, no secret sold, no seventh hearing, no sale.

**Ch 43 (bonds).** Through Ember 1–3 nothing changed hands under the Vail roof and nobody said what the number counted. Sella gave every instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom and the running water and never to the basket, said *the house*, kept her half-pace, glanced once at the clay jar without touching it, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and let her hand rest a half-beat longer at the setting; on the morning of Day 180, before the kiln, she touched the widow's mark, held it one breath, lifted the clay lid and tipped the carried coal under a broken pot, and did not touch the mark again at any shelf, well, rack, road, basket, afternoon or dusk, and nobody praised the opening or saw what it mended. Tarn took the broom only after it was instructed, six counted where the house can check and the seventh held where it cannot, the ear-sentence at his teeth on all three days and no further, nine stoppings still nine objects, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled; Jorra gave the men who came before first grey on Days 181 and 182 no name, kept his dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, withheld the full note from a High Kiln kiln-master on the wrong wind, and held his bent finger in the cold damper until Sella's sentence reached the pot, the fraction held and unspoken. The three held changes stayed on three separate places, the two shut-room tellings at two, the charcoal unread and the two rooms that do not touch untouched. No bond paid or spoken, no name given, no secret sold and no payer named.

**Ch 44 (bonds).** Through Ember 4–6 nothing changed hands under the Vail roof and nobody said what the number counted. Sella gave every instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom and the running water and never to the basket, said *the house*, kept her half-pace, glanced once at the clay jar cold under its glaze without touching it, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand with the half-beat at setting, sent a neighbour's cost to the pot and the weather, and left the widow's mark where it hung, because a morning and a shelf are not openings and the one opening was before the kiln on Ember 1. Tarn took the broom only after it was instructed, six counted where the house can check and the seventh held at the lip, Garl's unforged sentence beside the knife, the ear-sentence short of the word that would call the loan on all three days, nine stoppings still nine objects with no tenth before the seventh year was earned, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled. His one act of the week was private and unreconciled: on Day 183 he took the warm stone out of its pot in the Debt Loft, set it on the sill in the light, looked at it and put it back, still cold, not forgiven; the boy heard only the scrape of a pot's foot from a room he cannot enter and kept it on a fourth place of its own. Jorra gave the men who came before first grey on Days 184 and 185 no name, kept his dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, withheld the full note from a man on a wrong wind who said a pot singing early generally sings itself out early, no pot lost, and had his bent finger standing in the cold damper on both days as a remembered holding and not a new change. The three held changes stayed on three separate places, the two shut-room tellings at two, the charcoal unread and the two rooms that do not touch untouched. No bond paid or spoken, no name given, no secret sold, no payer named and no new scar, possession or change of voice.

**Ch 45 (bonds).** Through Ember 7–9 nothing changed hands under the Vail roof and nobody said what the number counted. Sella gave every instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom and the running water and never to the basket, said *the house*, kept her half-pace, glanced once at the clay jar cold under its glaze without touching it, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand with the half-beat at setting, and left the widow's mark where it hung, because a morning, a shelf, a rack and a dusk are not openings and the one opening was before the kiln on Ember 1. On Day 187 a neighbour with a wet pot asked what the firing cost; she sent her to the pot and the weather with *Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it*, and nothing was priced, refused or entered. On Day 188 she set the basket at the yard's edge, turned his face as she would turn a pot to the light and went wide around the place the carriers crossed, giving no name for what ailed the man. Tarn kept six counted and the seventh held, brought the ear-sentence to the threshold of the word that calls the loan on all three days and stopped with no figure and no name, his stroke over the flagstones without the half-check and Garl's unforged sentence beside the knife; the loan stayed uncalled. Jorra gave the men in the back room no name, knew the full note of a pot three turns ahead and withheld it from a man on a wrong wind, and held his bent finger in the cold damper until her unfinished sentence reached the pot, the fraction a remembered holding and not a new change; the two silences stayed unjoined. The old life stayed offstage and no bond changed hands: no name given, no tin or box opened, no figure paid, no collector called.

**Ch 46 (bonds).** Through Ember 10–12 nothing changed hands under the Vail roof and nobody said what the number counted. Sella gave every instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom, the running water and the river wind and never to the basket, said *the house* and *The house will keep it*, kept her half-pace and her jar-glance without a touch, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand with the half-beat at setting, and left the mark where it hung, because a market and a reading are not openings and the one opening was before the kiln on Ember 1. On Day 189 she stood half a pace off at the moot-post shade through the reading and the bell; on Days 190 and 191 women's cost-questions went to the pot and the weather. Berr read Ember's first *one in twelve, as the notice says* over the basket, said *The levy is called. It is not called by me* and *your house*, never *I*, and went off up the clay road with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, believing he had done a kindness; Hessa carried his slate up the road edge-on and unread. The reading did not answer the man carried out on Day 188: no figure, collector or slate knew what he cost, and the pale line stayed on a place of its own. Tarn held six counted and the seventh at the lip, the ear-sentence short of the word that calls the loan, nine stoppings with no tenth and the Seed 16 breath unspent; Jorra's dust and withheld note, his remembered holding at the damper and the two shut-room tellings stood as they had. No name given, no tin or box opened, no collector called and no payer named.

**Ch 48 (bonds).** With Sella nothing changed hands across three days: the instruction to the wool, fire, pot, broom and running water and never the basket, *the house*, the half-pace, the jar-glance without a touch, gruel from the house's own dish with his mouth unwiped, the pot handle two inches beyond his hand with the half-beat at setting, the mark untouched, and the two rooms that do not touch. With Tarn the equality held at the place it had moved to — six counted and the seventh at the lip, the ear-sentence at the threshold of the word that would call the loan, nine stoppings with no tenth, the Seed 16 breath unspent, no collector — and the boy did not join his own held inch to Tarn's held breath. With Jorra the dust and the withheld note and the finger waiting in the damper stood as before, *he's not Vail* sealed and the promise ungiven, and the sweet edge on the wrong wind was not made to answer the man carried out on Day 188. The old life stayed offstage: no present-tense break, no `\memory{}` tag, no name looked for. No bond paid, sold or spoken; no new scar or possession.

**Ch 51 (bonds).** With Sella nothing changed hands across Days 204–206: every instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom, the running water and the river wind and never the basket, *the house*, the half-pace, the jar-glance and hand shut half over nothing without a touch, gruel from the house's own dish with his mouth unwiped, the pot handle two inches beyond his hand with a half-beat at the setting and no look toward the bed, her lips along a two-gapped row with no figure, her finger kept from beneath the charcoal, the mark untouched, and the two rooms that do not touch. She came to the fire before the basket on all three mornings and was gone to her own bed before the small hours of the last day. With Tarn the equality under the roof held where it had moved — six counted where the house can check and the seventh at the lip, the ear-sentence brought to the word that would call the loan and stopped with no figure and no name, the Seed 16 breath unspent, nine stoppings nine with no tenth, no collector — and the boy did not lay his own held inch across Tarn's held breath or the three held changes. With Jorra a word came up from Slate Row that a slate had burned in the night, and the boy filed it on a place of its own beside the dust and the two shut-room tellings with a hand between them and wrote no cause across them, while Jorra grew quieter, *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name stayed ungiven. Garl was kept in objects — the salt coat, the hollow seat, the clean dark line and finer grey, the proverb gone to the pot, the uncalled debt against the tiles — with no lesson made and no collector called. The old life stayed offstage: no tag, no present-tense break. No name given, no debt paid, no new scar or possession.

**Ch 52 (bonds).** Through the kiln month's last working week Sella still carried him at the half-pace from every open slate, gave every instruction to the wool, fire, pot, broom and river wind and never to the basket, said *the house*, set the pot handle two inches beyond his hand, moved her lips along a two-gapped row without a figure, kept her finger from beneath the charcoal and left the widow's mark untouched at every shelf, market, road, afternoon and dusk — no opening in twenty-nine days; his mouth stayed unwiped and the thread at his mouth frozen, and the two rooms did not touch. Garl was kept to the last day in objects and not in a story — salt coat, hollow seat, unswept clean dark line and finer grey, proverb gone to the pot, the debt against the tiles uncalled and worse for being uncalled — with no lesson made and no collector called, his name unspoken for two hundred and nine days. Tarn brought the ear-sentence to the word that would call the loan and stopped, nine stoppings with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, the Seed 16 breath unspent, his stroke over the flagstones without the half-check; Jorra's bent finger held in the cold damper until Sella's sentence reached the pot as a remembered repetition, and the burned-slate word came a second time as a direction joined to no cause. Berr carried the third reading up the clay road in his locked box, Hessa carried the slate up it unread, and neither the clerk's inward measure nor the runner's outward one was joined to any keeping. No bond changed hands, no name was given, no tin, box or slate was opened, and the old life stayed offstage.

**Ch 53 (bonds).** Through the turning of Ember into Grain nothing changed hands and nothing was said across three lived days. Sella still carried him at the half-pace from every open slate and at the going of the light went around the pan's gone ring, the bare trough, the rack space with its broken birth-slates, the field's empty place and the place the carriers crossed with a look and a wide step and no number; every instruction went to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom, the running water and the river wind and never to the basket, and the widow's mark hung where it hung with no opening in thirty, thirty-one and thirty-two days. With Tarn the equality held where it had moved: six counted where the house can check and the seventh at the lip, the ear-sentence brought to the word that would call the loan and stopped with no figure and no name, nine stoppings with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, the Seed 16 breath unspent, no collector on any morning; his stroke over the flagstones kept without the half-check. With Jorra the two silences stayed unjoined: men unnamed went low into the back room before first grey on all three mornings and got no name, his bent finger stood in the cold damper until Sella's sentence reached the pot as a remembered repetition, *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name stayed ungiven. Garl was kept in objects at the turning of the month — salt coat, hollow seat, two unswept lines, proverb gone to the pot, the debt against the tiles circled without a name — no lesson made and no collector called, his name unspoken for two hundred and twelve days. Berr's box stayed locked up the road, Hessa's slate unread and the soft-cuffed clerk set for his uncalled measure, all offstage and none reached back; Dessa stayed dead and unnamed, Marra out of Sill, the old life offstage without a tag or a present-tense break. No bond paid or spoken, no name given, no new scar or possession.

**Ch 55 (bonds).** Sella still carried him at the half-pace from every open slate and set him on his bed under the roof, not the Debt Loft, with the fold put right without a look at his face and the thread left frozen; she went to her own bed before the small hours of Day 218 and the two rooms did not touch. Tarn still kept six where the house could check and the seventh at the lip, brought the ear-sentence to the word that would call the loan and stopped with no figure and no name; no collector came on Grain 7, 8 or 9. Jorra's quiet went before him into the shed the way a cold air goes before a door, the household straightening around it without an order; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the slate-burners neither given nor withdrawn. Garl stayed in objects with the tile debt neither paid nor settled nor named, and the widow's mark was untouched for a thirty-sixth, thirty-seventh and thirty-eighth day. Hessa's slate, Berr's box, the clerk's uncalled measure, Dessa's tin, Marra, Fenna and Ulda stayed offstage: the seventh green and the seventh sighting are owed at the first market of Grain on Day 219 as a colour and a shape, and neither came early. No bond changed hands, no name was spoken over the boy and no payer was named.

**Ch 57 (bonds at the close).** With Sella nothing changed hands: she carried him up and down the clay road at the going of the light, set him on his bed under the roof and not the Debt Loft, put the fold right without a look at his face, gave every instruction to the wool, fire, pot, broom, running water and river wind and never to the basket, and has still not said whose child he is. With Tarn the two ears kept their equality and silence, the loan uncalled and the seventh year still held; with Jorra the bent finger stood in the damper as a remembered repetition and *he's not Vail* stayed sealed. Garl stayed in objects, Dessa stayed dead and unnamed, Marra stayed offstage, and the old life did not break through. No bond given, named, opened or paid.

**Ch 58 (bonds).** With Sella nothing changed hands across the dressing of the grain: the half-pace at the going of the light, the bed under the roof and not the Debt Loft, the fold put right without a look at his face, every instruction to the wool, fire, pot, broom, running water and river wind and never to the basket, and still no word about whose child he is. She gave every cost-question to the pot and the weather; the year's first grain came out of its covering, was beaten and winnowed and spread and poured back under wool, dressed and stored, and the two keepings --- the grain under the fold and the place in the returning line --- were not laid together or given a figure. With Tarn the two ears kept their equality and silence, six counted and the seventh at the lip, the loan uncalled and no collector on any of the three days; with Jorra the bent finger stood in the damper as a remembered repetition, the burned-slate word held at four, and *he's not Vail* stayed sealed while the promise of the boy's name went neither given nor withdrawn. Garl stayed in objects and unspoken, Dessa's cost unentered, Sella's private roll unshown, the widow's mark untouched in forty-five, forty-six and forty-seven days. Marra, Berr, Hessa, Alda and the foundling stayed offstage; no bond given, named, opened, spent or paid.

**Ch 59 (bonds).** Nothing changed hands with anyone across Days 228–230: Sella carried him at the half-pace from every open slate, set the basket in the moot-post's thin shade on Grain 20 and carried him home saying nothing of what had been read over him, and in the other room kept her own counted breaths to herself behind a wall that does not touch his; Tarn kept six and the seventh and the ear-sentence stopped short of the calling word with no collector on any of the three roads; Jorra kept the men unnamed, the full note withheld and *he's not Vail* sealed; Berr went off up the clay road with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, believing he had done a kindness; Hessa carried the slate up unread. No name was given, no loan called, no tin, box or slate opened, no seventh hearing, no tenth stopping and no fifth telling of the burned slate; the new question about the row has no owner yet.

**Ch 60 (bonds).** Nothing changed hands with anyone across Days 231–233. Sella came to the fire before the basket each morning, gave every instruction to the wool, pot, broom, water, fire and river wind and never to the basket, said *the house* and *The house will keep it*, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand with a half-beat and no look toward the bed, and left the widow's mark untouched — fifty-one, fifty-two and fifty-three days without an opening; she heard the second asking go over the boards and answered it with the pot and the weather, sending the cost-questions the same way, and gave no figure for the covered grain. Tarn kept six where the house could check and the seventh at the lip, the Seed 16 breath unspent, the loan uncalled and no collector on Grain 22, 23 or 24; he brought the ear-sentence to the calling word three times as a remembered stopping, and the warm stone lay cold in its loft pot. Jorra parted dust from slip as clot from vessel, kept dusty and clean forearms on one tray, withheld his pot's full note and let the three sayings, the four tellings of the burned slate and the two tellings of the shut room stand where they stood; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name was neither given nor withdrawn. Garl stayed in objects with his name unspoken two hundred and thirty-three days. Berr, Hessa, the clerk, the green-mouthed woman and the foundling stayed offstage; the old life stayed offstage too. No name, witness, payer or figure came, no seventh hearing, tenth stopping or fifth telling stood up, and the two askings on the seventh place were not joined into a hearing, a cause or a name.

**Ch 61 (bonds).** Nothing changed hands with anyone across Days 234–236. Sella came to the fire before the basket each morning, gave every instruction to the wool, fire, pot, broom, running water and river wind and never to the basket, said *the house* and *The house will keep it*, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand with a half-beat and no look toward the bed, turned the dressed grain under wool with no figure, and left the widow's mark untouched — fifty-four, fifty-five and fifty-six days without an opening; when the ownerless question came over the boards a third time on the afternoon of Grain 26 she answered it with the pot and the weather, the weather first and the price of barley after it, and nobody leaned in to take the answer down. Tarn kept six where the house could check and the seventh at the lip, the Seed 16 breath unspent, the loan uncalled and no collector on Grain 25, 26 or 27; he brought the ear-sentence to the calling word as a remembered stopping on each day, and the warm stone lay cold in its loft pot. Jorra parted dust from slip as clot from vessel, withheld his pot's full note, kept the burned-slate word at four and the shut-room tellings at two, and let the three askings stand on their seventh place without claiming them, supplying a witness or making a cause; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name was neither given nor withdrawn. Garl stayed in objects with his name unspoken two hundred and thirty-six days. Berr, Hessa, the clerk, the green-mouthed woman and the foundling stayed offstage; the old life stayed offstage too. No witness, row, payer or figure came, no seventh hearing, tenth stopping or fifth telling stood up, and the three askings on the seventh place were not joined into a hearing, a cause or a name.

## Arc So Far

He has lived four days, awake through most of them, in a body that can do nothing he wants. The double consciousness has staged itself in full: the clinical register reads hand-grips, pulses, the bend of a finger, the not-looking, the return of blood to cold extremities and the arithmetic of a bed and a loft; the infant body drools, hungers, cannot hold up its head, and wins every argument by being louder. On the fourth day he is carried out of the only warm room he knows into the Cross on Account-day, where two hundred people want a figure and Berr Clay gives them one. He hears his own kind priced in the figure and cannot turn his head far enough to see who prices them; nobody addresses him, and nobody taps a slate for him. He is cracked, low-capacity, unranked --- below Thin, twelve to fifteen embers at best --- with no Warm moved and no power used. Three facts have gone in and cannot be got out: a name was written on the slate before anyone saw his face; a household arranges its silences around a thing it will not say; and he has been given up by a mother who loves him and told he is dead by a woman who is lying to keep him safe. The clinical ear has travelled from a cup across the birth room to a pot in the Vail house to the district's own slate --- three vessels, four hearings by the afternoon of Stone 10 --- and remains a prisoner of two paces of trodden ash, with no Grathic word for the sound and no hand to lift toward it. The old life's grammar has gone outward: Ostrowski's two duties spoken into the market, Vesci's *that's what a chart is for* inside Berr's *kindness is entered*. The count has grown --- a hundred and nine steps, five then eight pigs, four ways out of the square, six hands, two hoop-strips, the bell's four-four-and-a-gap --- and every number is useless and untaxable. He has passed from a room where he was kept without explanation into a square where he was counted without being addressed. He keeps the count as the one entry in any room that belongs to nobody and cannot be entered on a slate. He cannot speak, cannot read Grathic, cannot reach a cup, cannot lift a hand two inches to his own chin, and is as lost as the reader. By the middle tend of Grain he is two hundred and twenty-four days old and the harvest has come in without a figure: the first cut grain lies covered near the hearth and the place in the returning line goes around with a look and no number, while the hand's last two-rhythm holding stays a remembered coordination on its sixth place, exact, complete and worth nothing (T071/T072, T098 advanced and unpaid, T061/T082).

On Days 5 and 6 the flat note acquires a fifth hearing and its first from a person: a pot under the Vail roof through the wall, then the dying neighbour at the Drawing, the fifth sound coming out of flesh that had been warm that morning. The figure read aloud at the Cross has become a measure taken from a body, entered in a neat ordinary hand beside a bed, while he lay in a basket at the edge of the light and could not reach the tube, read the slate or lift a knuckle toward either. He has added a second room to his private file of unexplained things --- the death-house with its Ash ridge a finger's width thicker than Vail's, turning the corner without a break --- and filed it beside the Vail ridge, the charred slate and the barrow of grey without joining any of them. He is six days old, cracked, mute, unranked, still below Thin; the pocket-place is reached twice more, the name unlooked-at, the nine promises of a boat carried as an absence. Nothing has been gained and no power has moved. The cost is the same and worse: knowing exactly, in two rooms in two days, and being the only adult in neither.

On Days 7, 8 and 9 he is seven, then eight, then nine days old, and for the first time somebody under the roof has witnessed his ear and chosen silence --- not safety and not yet a threat, an equality with no word in either man's mouth. He learns that a thing can be kept without happening: a pot turned toward and giving nothing leaves an interval with the shape of a sound, and he files it as carefully as if the sound had come. He learns that a number goes around a district as a closed fist and arrives in a house only as an absence, that a seventh year can be held back by a man who counts every other day, and that a woman can keep accounts in grey wool where no Court's thumb would think to look. He reads two not-lookings of the same shape --- the man who priced him and the woman who kept him --- and is put among the bits in a count he cannot know he is inside. The private file grows by a cord and a second cord, eel-bones, nineteen steps, a missing step, a crust and an interval, and stays unreadable by anyone else; the gap remains the part nobody has thought to enter. He knows exactly, in three rooms and three mornings, and can lift a hand to none of it: the crust lies warm in his fold and cannot be eaten. Still cracked, mute, unranked, below Thin; five hearings from four sources and no sixth; no power moved and nothing gained.

On Days 10, 11, 12, 13 and 14 he is ten through fourteen days old and the private file acquires a household scale: a kindling stack measured like a supply shelf, four, four and a space where the fifth should be; a second interval kept under the Vail roof when a pot turned toward gives nothing; the sign Alda makes at his wrist without knowing it and covers with a date; a neighbour's hand to a slate before her child; a hairline in river ice that runs and stops short of the edge; and a fourth sighting of the pause in the neat dark hand at the Court's table. He keeps the four pauses side by side and does not join them, keeps the Drawing collector's ordinary hand with no pause in its own place, and keeps the hearing count at five from four sources. He learns that a man can go still for two breaths at a kindness from the woman who holds his debt, that a loan that can hear is worth more than the debt that holds a man and therefore dangerous, that the house keeps days as loaves and glances and absences, and that four rooms --- birth room, kiln-house, death-house and Court's room --- hold the same bench, pot, draught and gap, which he refuses to turn into a cause. The crust has gone from warm to cold in the fold and is still uneaten. Fourteen days old, carried everywhere, mute, cracked, unranked, below Thin; the Five eleven days off and the household keeping the number badly while he keeps it exactly; no power moved and nothing gained.

On Days 15 through 24 he is fifteen through twenty-four days old and the household's arithmetic becomes a tend and then a year's last market. He learns that a number can arrive as a fist shut on a door-latch and a mouth that thinks better of speaking, that the district will give out three, then two, then whatever the moot has already decided to pay, while the days go one after another and one day after tonight is tomorrow. The flat note happens twice within reach and cannot be placed: a vessel carried past the door gives it on Stone 22, and a pot cracks all the way through two paces from his head on Stone 25 and is called a pot gone wrong, so the tray of intervals acquires a third object while the five hearings from four sources stay five. The pause in the neat dark hand is seen a fifth time at the market's edge on Stone 30, in one untagged beat of orange flare light on wet snow, and changes the count and nothing else: no power, no word, no hand, no standing. Two warmths lie side by side in the fold --- Mira's fresh crust and the spent one from Stone 15 --- and neither is entered or eaten; a gift that cannot be answered is a gift that cannot be refused, which he has learned in bread. He is carried down and back at a half-pace from every open slate, set in a finger's width of shade with cold iron at his cheek, and lies on his bed under the roof at dusk with the fire keeping four, four and a gap. Still cracked, mute, unranked, below Thin; no Warm moved, no clamp, no experiment; the Five are tomorrow and Kindling will come into the house from somewhere he cannot see or reach.

On Days 25 through 30 he is twenty-five, then thirty days old, and the count is the only thing that survives both the burning of the Ash and the opening of the slates. The Five come and go as named days with no market and no tend-day: a fire carried from hearth to hearth enters the house from somewhere he cannot see or reach, and he extends the count to its light with a hypothetical fifth that is not a sixth hearing; a clean institutional voice reads a whole return for somebody absent and a name the household will not speak, and he files the silence beside the kiln's missing breath; the Long Night gives him stories whose gaps have the shape of vessels' intervals and a bell told four, four and a gap which he keeps apart from the bell he heard; the Remembering gives him an unspoken name and a woman covering a sign with a date, while the pocket-place holds wool where a lit rectangle with a name had lain and the boat promise comes and goes in past tense, untagged. He learns that a thing left in place is still accounted for and that a thing burned and called dealt with is another way of keeping the same room. On the Last Day two crusts lie in the fold and no third is given; Sella measures the ridges with one thumb and leaves them, then burns the grey and calls it gone. On Ilmen 1 the mark is untouched because there is no opening, the new year's slates are opened elsewhere as a direction, one in twelve stays printed, and he carries five hearings from four sources and no sixth, five unjoined pauses, the told bell beside the heard bell and the empty fifth into Y.H. 813. He is cracked, mute, unranked, below Thin, carried everywhere; no power moved, no experiment, no clamp, no heresy, and the hand outside the wool goes up an inch toward the warmth and comes down.

On Days 31 through 37 he is thirty-one, then thirty-seven days old, and the thaw comes on in small things the district will not name. He learns that almost is its own kind of note: a pot answers nearly full and it is neither the flat answer of the five hearings nor a silence, and he files it as an answer to a long-kept interval rather than make a cause of it, because he has learned what three likenesses laid in a row will do in a better-lit room. He learns that a district can taste a change in its water and pass the word *honeyed* around without owning the day, and that a woman going thin can be called weather by everyone who loves her. The count stays exact for seven days and buys him a thumb on wicker he cannot spend and the knowledge of a vessel he cannot treat. Sella's instruction still goes to the fire and the wool; Alda's fourth note goes under a floorboard; Jorra's dust comes out of the back room; Tarn's head turns a degree and says nothing; Berr goes off with the box locked; Mira shows the second cord twisted twice and brings no crust. Nothing is joined, paid or explained; the sixth hearing and the hand-on-the-basket detail are held for Day 38.

On Days 38 through 44 he is thirty-eight, then forty-four days old, and the count buys him three things he cannot spend: a hearing, a shape and a word. A woman who loved him without knowing him goes into the clay-heavy brook and is lifted out by hands and no words; the surgeon reads exactly how a vessel cracked all the way through gives way and has an infant's hand that cannot reach the body. He pays the detail held since Ilmen 8 in past tense, untagged, and the old life arrives once and does not break into the present. No collector is called and nobody names the omission, so warmth left where it fell and the morning came --- the first edge of the heresy he will one day speak aloud. The district's arithmetic resumes over her unentered body: one in twelve printed and unchallenged, the true subtraction going over the basket, the number for the thaw passing from a fist to a mouth to a shoulder and never given. At House Silt he sees, from the height at which shelves are highest, a pewter tin above an empty bed with a pale fold at its seam and a thumb's bright rub on the lid, and enters it nowhere; Alda's turned slate, Mira's two cords and the tin on the shelf stand in three places and do not touch. At the Weir a sixth room is added, with a bench, a pot, a draught and a weak board in the plank walk, and he keeps it apart from the other five. Orra's knot and the word forty go on separate trays, and the watched question stays out of reach. Nothing is joined, paid or explained except a sound, a shape and a word, and the count still stands four, four and a gap.

On Days 45 through 47 he is forty-five, then forty-seven days old, and the count buys him a shape he can read and a taking he cannot spend. Below the north bridge a brine-gull goes off with a coal not yet cold and one warmth less is abroad in the district with no slate the poorer; he keeps the subtraction, which nobody else keeps. Orra's forty-first knot is now legible as a shape --- double turn, tail tucked, left over right, the small weight of a thumb --- but has no name in any tongue he can speak, and the word forty is not said again. Mira's two cords and Orra's knot-string stand on separate boards, and the six hearings stay six, five from four sources and one from a person, with no seventh. Under the roof Sella goes around the month in loaves and glances; then, in the afternoon of Day 46, the breath of barley smoke stops coming off the water and the smoky crust is gone --- taken quietly, eaten by nobody, named by nobody. He sets Sella's hand, Alda's hand and Mira's hand on three places on a tray and will not let the tray become a cause, because he has learned in a better-lit room what three likenesses laid in a row will do. The surgeon registers the empty place with the exactness of a wound and can do nothing with it. At the well frost writes on the stone lip where the Warm has lately gone out, a number passes from fist to mouth to shoulder and arrives nowhere, a weak board at the Weir is heard of and not trodden, and the clerk still sits at the Fishstairs for a measure nobody called. He keeps all of them in separate places and calls none of them a cause. Nothing has been joined, paid or explained; his hand outside the wool goes up an inch toward the warmth and comes down.

On Days 48 through 54 he is forty-eight, then fifty-four days old, and a seven-day passage buys him signatures he cannot spend. Frost writes itself back onto the well-lip in white fronds and the same branching shape runs down his own ribs under the wool; the oiled paper in the Vail window hangs slack in its frame; the neighbour who said the ice held and the man who called it honeyed both keep their counsel, and an argument that used to end in weather has no arguer left to end it. He sets the slack frame, the silence and the hairlines that stop short of the edge beside the honeyed ice and the higher brook in places that do not touch. At the Cross a figure is read over the basket and the true subtraction goes around it unspoken while the bell goes four, four and a gap; at the racks Jorra's dust comes back from the room off the shed; under the roof Alda writes a fifth note and covers the unaware sign with a date. A second pot answers nearly full and he files it beside the first, advanced and not paid, with no hand to tap either and no Grathic word for nearly. Mira's two cords and Orra's knot-string stand on boards that do not touch. More hairlines stop short of the edge, the Singing Sill's note comes early, and the weak board in the plank walk at the Weir is said plainly to be still there and trodden by nobody, held beside the missing step and the hairline and made into no accident. He carries one crust and the empty place beside it through all seven days without a name, keeps the six hearings beside and never among and the three hands on their tray, and his hand outside the wool goes up an inch toward the warmth and comes down. Nothing has been joined, paid, explained or ended.

On Days 55 through 60 he is fifty-five, then sixty days old --- two months --- and a six-day passage buys him a danger described in words by a stranger and still not recognised under his own roof. A man come downriver from Greybank tells the well that in the last war a counting room was shut and the men inside died of the breath with the damper closed; the yard takes it as history with a date and Sella tells the fire it is the thaw, while he files the saying beside the told bell, the heard bell and the kiln's four-four-and-a-gap and writes no cause. Three doors down he sees, from the basket's low angle, the pewter tin above the empty bed with its pale fold and thumb-bright lid, and in the same room Alda's tin shut inside her bodice: two private records in two materials, both unopened, neither askable. The house keeps its days badly in loaves and glances while he keeps the exact three days to Silt and cannot spend one; the yard prepares a sweeping already done, and Sella lays one thumb in the clean dark line and the same thumb in the ridge two doors down and fetches no broom. At the last market Berr reads one in twelve twice and removes himself from his own sentence, Mira answers a question with her own answer and touches a grey cord with a second cord twisted twice beneath it, neither knot counted, and brings no crust; a barrow of grey goes out unread. Silt comes in on mud. A new hairline of grey gathers in the Vail corner beside the old clean dark line and the house calls it thaw and will not sweep it; he sets the new grey and the left-in-place line in places that do not touch. The river stirs under the Ilm's skin and is called weather; the clerk stays set at the Fishstairs, the weak board stays untrodden, the one crust lies stiff in the fold with the empty place beside it carried into Silt without a name. He is sixty days old, carried everywhere, mute, cracked, unranked, below Thin; six hearings stay six, no seventh comes, no sixth note is written, and his hand outside the wool goes up an inch toward the new grey and comes down. Nothing has been joined, paid, explained or ended.

On Days 61 through 67 he is sixty-one, then sixty-seven days old, and a seven-day passage buys him signs of a flood nobody will own. He learns that water going over water has a note of its own, a low turning with no vessel in it, and that the district's one word for it is *weather*, which does not name the water but grants permission to stop looking at it; that a district can say *honeyed all through* to a set of boards and let nobody own the saying; that a debt left lying is not a debt forgiven, only a debt nobody has picked up; that a man can move a damper with his anger and let the household straighten around the silence; that a sixth page can be written and turned down and change nothing for the woman who writes it or the child it is written about; and that the pale stone can go out of a fold and be left unmentioned by everyone. The near-full answer repeats a third time and stays advanced and not paid; three intervals stay objects; six hearings stay six with no seventh; the word stays a saying with the double turn's shape and the weight of a thumb apart from both. Four low sounds stay in four places and the river's note is a fifth thing that is not a hearing. The weak board stays trodden by nobody, the clerk stays set, the new grey stays finer than the old line and unswept, and the flood is four days off. He keeps the count through all seven days and crosses into Silt 9 unspent. Still cracked, mute, unranked, carried everywhere, with one crust in the fold and the empty place beside it, no power moved and no experiment made; his hand outside the wool goes up an inch toward the water and comes down.

On Days 68 through 74 he is sixty-eight, then seventy-four days old, and a seven-day passage buys him the note of a whole district under strain and a cold he cannot warm. The flood comes up the Fishstairs in a thin brown sheet, stands on the second step at the Cross on Account-day, goes around the clean dark line at the Vail corner and the burned line two doors down, and goes off into mud; the district calls it weather and then the thaw that was, while he files the going beside the coming and writes no cause. Beneath the four low sounds he hears a low turning of many small failures inside one slow certain one --- a vessel of enormous capacity with a thin place nobody will name --- and has no word and no hand to point, so he files it too. Below the sill a child comes up from the water with a foot that stays cold; the surgeon in him reads the extremity going before the core and the afterdrop waiting on the far side, and the infant in him cannot put a hand two paces out, and no cause or cure is spoken. He watches Berr's printed figure go over the basket while the true subtraction goes unspoken, Mira's two cords and Orra's knot-string stand unread, Alda's sixth page and the pewter tin stay shut, Jorra's dusty forearms come and go, Tarn's seventh year press to its end, and Sella's broom go around both lines without either being swept. Six hearings stay six, three near-full answers stand advanced, the word and the knot's shape and the weight of a thumb stay apart, the four low sounds stay four with the river's note a fifth thing, and the count crosses into Silt 16 unspent. Still cracked, mute, unranked, carried everywhere, no power moved and no experiment made; nothing joined, paid, explained or ended.

On Days 75 through 77 he is seventy-five, then seventy-seven days old, and the count becomes the only honest arithmetic in a district that sweeps around what it cannot enter. The new life offers him two objects he cannot spend: a head turned half a beat after the knock by a child who cannot say why, and a warm loaf passed from a man from Slate Row to a pocket that holds no slate. He keeps them apart, as he keeps his own turning apart from the child's, because a name laid on a thing too early closes the chart on it. The surgeon's register reads the recovery as a ward walking around a bed; the infant wants the crust and the breast and the bucket and can reach none. The half-beat is held for the day it lands as the physical tell of a betrayal, and the loaf has already gathered interest in an account he cannot read. Nothing joined, paid, explained or ended.

On Days 78 through 84 he is seventy-eight, then eighty-four days old, and a seven-day passage buys him a second telling of a danger nobody will join. The lean week is the district counting what the water took and finding that the counting changes nothing: Account-day is read over fewer bodies and the printed figure stands unchallenged, the gone board at the Weir is gone or trodden flat and nobody has been down to see, the honeyed ice and the stopping hairlines and the early Sill note are spoken of only past tense as *the thaw that was*, and the fire under blind ash keeps four, four and a gap with its payer still unnamed. A second man come downriver from Greybank tells the bucket-round the saying of the counting room shut in the last war with the damper closed and the men inside dead of the breath, eighteen leagues east and dated; he files the second telling on the same tray as the first without letting the two touch, keeping the likeness and the difference — breath through the nose against breath in the throat, both saying *not the war's hand* — and writes no cause across either. The surgeon's register reads the cold foot below the sill, Tarn's held breath, the district going thin and the anatomy of the sealed room as exactly as it ever read a chart, and none of it can be spent. He wants the crust, the breast, the bucket two inches beyond his hand and his number spoken by somebody who would know what it counted, and gets none and can ask for none. At dusk Sella sets him on his bed under the roof and not in the Debt Loft, puts the fold right without looking at his face and leaves the thread at his mouth frozen and unwiped; the river's note comes through the finger's-width-open door and the fire's missing breath comes through the floor, kept in two separate places as they have been for eighty-four days. One brown-edged crust lies stiff in the fold, the empty place beside it still unnamed. Nothing joined, paid, explained or ended; the count crosses into Silt 26 unspent.

On Days 85 through 90 he is eighty-five, then ninety days old, three months at the close, and a six-day passage carries his exact count across the turn of Silt into Seed while the house keeps its arithmetic in loaves, glances and a space nobody names. Silt goes out on the same mud it came in on: the yard goes around the brown skin holding a boot's print, the clean dark line, the new finer grey called the thaw and the space under the lowest rack with a broom and a glance and no figure spoken. He keeps the two tellings of the shut counting room on their tray beside the told bell, the heard bell, the early Sill note and the kiln's gap, likeness and difference kept and neither joined to any kiln, and no third telling comes. At the wheel-shed Jorra's grey dust and his bent finger at the damper go unasked; Tarn's knife sits on its sill with six counted and the seventh nearly gone and still held. Three doors down Alda lays her birth-slates before the fold, presses it too long, crosses the flower unknowing and covers the sign with the date she gave a woman more mornings dead than any living person keeps count of, saying *warm and breathing* exact and not true, her tin and her sixth page both shut. At the Fishstairs Mira answers a question with her own answer and shows the second cord twisted twice beneath the grey with neither knot counted, her board beside Orra's without touching; the soft-cuffed clerk still sits for an uncalled measure and the market walks over the out-of-line landing without a fall. On the last market of Silt Berr wipes his thumb twice, reads one in twelve twice over the basket, takes himself out of his own sentence and goes off toward High Kiln with the box locked, believing he has done a kindness; a foundling turns his head toward a pot a half-beat after the boy's and the boy files the delay without a cause. On Seed 1 the first work of the planting month is sweeping, not planting; one crust lies stiff in the fold with the empty place beside it still unnamed and no third, the carried fire keeps four, four and a gap with its payer unnamed for ninety days, six hearings stay six, three near-full answers stand advanced and not paid, the word forty is unspoken, and the loan that can hear stays uncalled. The surgeon's register reads a whole square closed over a figure as a chart closed over a note, and the joke has never been funny enough to say aloud. At dusk Sella sets him on his bed under the roof, puts the fold right without a look at his face and leaves the thread frozen; the river's note comes through the finger's-width-open door and the fire's gap through the floor in two places as they have for ninety days. His hand goes up an inch toward the water and comes down. Nothing joined, paid, explained or ended; the count crosses into Seed unspent.

On Days 91 through 93 he is carried at the edge of the first planting week, ninety-one, ninety-two and ninety-three days old, and the count goes on being the only exact arithmetic in Low Kiln. The direction he carried for a month becomes worked ground: seed goes into the strips east of Low Kiln out of fists nobody counts, with brooms and glances and no figure spoken, while the house keeps its days in loaves pushed along a shelf, a glance at the clay jar and a space at the row's end nobody names. The old life stays offstage --- no Chicago break, no tagged memory --- and the surgeon's register keeps working perfectly and buying nothing: a fist of seed read as a surgeon sorting twenty bodies in ninety seconds, a damper read as the same iron bite that holds a vessel whole and takes a hand off a man, a cold foot read as an extremity going before the core, all exact and worth nothing to a body that cannot lift a hand to its own chin. Berr's twice-wiped thumb, Sella's instruction to the fire and never the basket, Jorra's bent finger ahead of the sentence, Tarn's sixth year held, Mira's two cords and Alda's date over a death all go onto their separate trays and are not joined. He wants his number said aloud by somebody who would know what it counted and nobody has any use for it; nobody has told him he is ninety-three days old. Nothing is joined, paid, explained or ended; his hand goes up an inch toward the water and comes down.

**Ch 20 (relationships at the close).** Sella still says *the house* where another woman would say *I*, gives every instruction to the fire, the wool, the bucket and the broom and never to the basket, carries him at the half-pace from every open slate, sets him on his bed under the roof and not in the Debt Loft, and has not said whose child he is; the wall has acquired no door. Alda crossed two fingers at the flower's red edge on Seed 8 before she knew it and covered the sign with the exact date she gave Dessa, saying *Warm and breathing, that's all I've set down*, which is exact and not true; her sixth page and both tins stayed shut. Mira answered a question about the run with her own answer on Seed 8, moved eel-bones while deciding whether to lie, put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure and showed the second cord twisted twice beneath it with neither knot counted and the knot for the thin child upriver untouched; her board lay beside Orra's and did not touch it, and her not-looking was a keeping. Jorra came through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door on Seed 7 with grey slate dust past the bent finger from men he did not name, spoke to the wheel and a pot, and put that finger into the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name was neither given nor withdrawn. Tarn said *Yes, house* to an early thaw and a late and kept wanting the late because it puts off the paying, kept six counted and the seventh nearly gone and held, went over the collector's road without turning and did not turn his head at vessels' notes; the loan that can hear stayed uncalled. Berr read one in twelve twice over the basket on Account-day, said it was not called by him, took himself out of his own sentence and went off with the box locked believing he had done a kindness. Ulda was glimpsed unnamed at the market's edge on Seed 9: a head half a beat after the knock, a green sliver rubbed, an unfinished sentence, no loaf and no slate. The man from Slate Row with soft hands was filed apart from Marra, the Drawing collector, the Court-table clerk and the Fishstairs clerk, apart from the child and the loaf, and was not called a sale. Marra is not in Sill and was not mistaken for any walk-on.

On Days 94 through 100 he is ninety-four, then one hundred days old --- three months and ten days --- and a seven-day passage at the cap buys him a round number nobody will say and a second absence nobody will count. The planting has become waiting: a fist lets seed go and comes back with chaff in its creases, which he reads as a chart closed with a neat line and a signature, exact and worth nothing. On Seed 6 a loaf's worth goes off the shelf with no scrape, crumb or print, so the row stands with two empty places where one used to stand, and he sets the shelf's new gap and the empty place in the fold on two places that do not touch and writes no cause across the space between them. The hundredth day comes and goes unmarked; he wants somebody who would know what his number counted to say it, and nobody does. At the Cross on Account-day a figure is read over him twice and does not know his number; at the Slate Row fringe a man with soft hands hands a figure to a slate and is filed apart from Marra, the Drawing collector, the Court-table clerk and the Fishstairs clerk, and apart from the child and the loaf --- not a sale. At the market's edge a foundling's head goes half a beat after his toward a pot, opens on the tail of a sentence and stops, and the delay is filed after the knock and not before it, not a hearing and not a seventh. Under the roof the private file is checked and unchanged: six hearings beside and never among, three near-full answers advanced, the word forty unspoken, four low sounds with the river's note a fifth, the two tellings of the shut room joined to no kiln, the damper's double bite filed as an object, the loan uncalled. Sella turns the coals with the back of a broken pot, tells the fire it is the thaw, puts the fold right without looking at his face and leaves the thread frozen; the carried fire's payer has gone unnamed for a hundred days. Nothing is joined, paid, explained or ended. No present-tense break, no tagged memory, no experiment, no clamp, no rank change, no new scar or possession; the hand outside the wool goes up an inch toward the water and comes down with the count at one hundred, unspent.

On Days 101 through 103 he is one hundred and one, then one hundred and two, then one hundred and three days old — three months and thirteen days — and the three days buy him a witness he cannot use. A neighbour names two absences in one breath, the shelf's old space as a thing the planting brought in and the fold's gap as a child's appetite, without seeing that they are one absence; he files the two tellings on two places and will not write a cause across the space, and T097 stands planted for a far-off finger that will one day lay them together and call them an account. The shelf keeps two unnamed gaps and the fold one crust and one unnamed gap, in rooms that do not touch, and no third crust comes. He learns that agreeing with both sides of an argument leaves a man wanting the late thaw because it puts off the paying, that a mention with a date in it is not a telling and no third telling is owed, that a household straightens around a silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted, and that a mercy-lie can curdle all the way through to a fact in a dead woman's face that no living voice can correct. A pot lifted a hand's breadth too high on Seed 12 comes up to the rim of a note and goes back down: the six hearings stay six beside and never among, no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the word forty unspoken with its readable shape and the weight of a thumb apart, four low things in four places with the river's note a fifth. The old life stays offstage; the surgeon's register reads the hand to the board before the eye to the boy, the twice-wiped thumb, the damper's double bite and the dark foot below the sill, and can spend none of it. Carried everywhere and the only adult in every room, he wants the number of his days said by somebody who would know what it counted and gets loaves, glances and an empty place at the row's end. Nothing joined, paid, explained or ended; the count stands at one hundred and three, unspent.

On Days 104 through 106 he is one hundred and four, then one hundred and five, then one hundred and six days old — three months and fourteen through sixteen days — and the direction he has carried for a month at last becomes worked ground with bodies in it, still counted in glances and handfuls and never in a figure. The hands the strips were said to want go in, and a man opens his mouth on the number and thinks better of it, so the district's withholding is performed inside the planting itself; the boy sets the direction beside the number and keeps them on two places, and the keeping produces nothing. He learns that a near-saying of a figure is not the figure, that a mercy-lie can curdle all the way through to a fact in a dead woman's face that no living voice can correct, and that a house which keeps its days in loaves can go on warm on an account nobody will own while its fire answers four low pulls and the silence where the fifth should have been. Tarn's seventh year presses to the very edge of the sill and is still held; the clerk set at the Fishstairs for a measure nobody called sits ahead of a death; the neighbour's one-breath naming advances T097 toward the far-off finger without being paid. Six hearings stay six beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers stand advanced and not paid, the word forty is unspoken, and the two shelf gaps and the fold's gap remain in rooms that do not touch. The file is exact, complete and worthless; the only adult in every room is the one with the least use in it. Nothing joined, paid, explained or ended; the count stands at one hundred and six, unspent.

On Days 107 through 109 he is one hundred and seven, then one hundred and eight, then one hundred and nine days old — three months and nineteen days — and the planting month enters its last tend while Account-day reads the figure over the very ground the hands went into on Seed 15; the count he keeps reaches one hundred and nine and buys nothing. He watches a house hold one breath across three days: Tarn's seventh year pressed to the very edge of the sill, the breath unspent, the unforged collector sentence kept where the seventh year is kept, the loan that can hear still uncalled. He watches Alda lay a date over a birth-flower sign and a neighbour at the loaf shelf name one absence twice without seeing it is one, and files the two tellings on two places beside the watcher's late turn as a third shape, joining none of them. Under the roof the private file comes back unchanged — six hearings beside and never among, three near-full answers advanced, three intervals, the word forty unspoken, the damper's double bite filed as an object, the river's note and the kiln's gap in two places — and he is the only adult in the room and the least use in it. The surgeon's register reads the held breath as a breath held against a clamp, the self-removing figure as a hand taken out of a field before the dressing goes on, and the delay after the knock as an object that comes after and is not a hearing; all of it exact, complete and worth nothing. Still cracked, mute, unranked, below Thin; no power moved, no experiment, no recognition, no ending, and his hand goes up an inch toward the note of water over water and comes down with the count unspent.

On Days 110 through 112 he is one hundred and ten, then one hundred and eleven, then one hundred and twelve days old — three months and twenty-two days — and the planting month's last tend buys him a third shaping of the absence nobody will join and the first small fraction of change in the equality under the roof. A neighbour at the loaf shelf makes a cause of two bare boards and never sees the empty place at his ear; he sets her one-breath saying beside the two earlier tellings and the watcher's late turn, three shapes of one kept thing on three places that do not touch, advanced and unpaid, waiting for a far-off finger that is not his. At the wheel a man's bent finger stands in the cold damper and a debt's broom checks half a beat behind it, the first time the two silences under the roof have moved against each other, and neither man turns or speaks; the loan that can hear is still uncalled. He learns that a stroke stopped where a stroke should have gone on is an interval like any other, that an account made out of two absences is a cause with a clean hand at the foot of it, and that the clean hand is the one thing he has spent two lives declining to become. Three doors down a dead woman's date still lies over a midwife's sign, and at the Fishstairs a woman's two cords and a Court clerk's uncalled measure keep their separate places. He checks the file as a man lays instruments on a towel and finds it unchanged, then adds what the three days gave without entering any of it. Still cracked, mute, unranked, below Thin, carried everywhere, the only adult in every room and the least use in it; no power moved, no experiment, no recognition. His hand goes up an inch toward the note of water over water and comes down with the count at one hundred and twelve, unspent.

On Days 113 through 115 he is one hundred and thirteen, then one hundred and fourteen, then one hundred and fifteen days old — three months and twenty-three to twenty-five days — and the last tend of the planting buys him a fourth shaping of the absence nobody will join and the first fraction of change held and not repeated. A man brings a number out of a fist toward the well and puts it back in his sleeve; a neighbour lays two rooms under one cause in one breath while he keeps three places apart; the witness advances and is not paid. Under the wheel a bent finger leads at the damper and a debt's stroke goes on over the flagstones without the check it made the day before, the stopped place kept on the stones and neither man naming the other's silence, so the equality under the roof has moved once and holds there. He learns that a held thing gathers shapes without becoming an account, that a change not repeated is still a change, and that a file done with perfect exactness buys nothing for a body that cannot close two inches on a fold. Three doors down a dead woman's date lies over a midwife's sign; at the Fishstairs a woman's two cords and a Court clerk's uncalled measure keep their places; at the Cross a soft-handed man holds a figure where nobody else can see it. He checks the file as a man lays instruments on a towel and finds it unchanged, then adds what the three days gave without entering any of it. Still cracked, mute, unranked, below Thin, carried everywhere, the only adult in every room and the least use in it; no power moved, no experiment, no recognition, no ending, and the count stands at one hundred and fifteen, unspent.

On Days 116 through 119 the planting month goes out and he carries its last absence into Reed without a name put to it. The hands come out of the strips east of Low Kiln on Seed 27, counted in glances and handfuls and never in a figure, and one hand that went in on Seed 15 does not come out with the rest; the yard goes around the place with a look and no number, and he files it as a chart closed with a neat line over a note nobody has opened (T098 planted). He keeps the field's empty place on a place of its own, not laid beside the shelf's two gaps, the fold's one crust and its empty place, or the word forty, because a likeness laid on a likeness grows a cause as surely as silt gathers behind a weir. On Seed 27 and again on Seed 29 neighbours lay two bare boards under one appetite in a single breath without seeing they have joined two rooms; the hearing of the joining is not the making of it, and he does not make it his own. On Seed 28 he reads the damper's double bite once more as the iron that holds and the iron that takes, sees Jorra's bent finger lead at it while Tarn's stroke goes on over the stones without the half-check, and keeps two silences unjoined; across the market a silent woman's green at the mouth is filed beside Berr's and the Silt 30 green, three shapes in three places, no name and no ear. On Seed 30 Berr reads one in twelve over the very absence nobody entered and takes himself out of his own sentence with *your house*, while the clerk stays set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called. At the going of the light Sella sets him on his bed under the roof and not in the Debt Loft, puts the fold right without looking at his face and leaves the thread frozen; the river's note comes through the finger's-width gap and the kiln's four, four and a gap through the floor, and the two counts do not meet. One hundred and nineteen stands unspent and spoken by nobody, and his hand goes up an inch toward the note of water over water and comes down. Nothing is joined, paid, named or ended; the hand that did not come back is held for a later body or figure.

On Days 120 through 122 he is one hundred and twenty, then one hundred and twenty-one, then one hundred and twenty-two days old — four months through four months and two days — and the planting month gives way to the reed month without a boundary being drawn. The work becomes a direction and a going-downriver, judged by the arm that carries it, and the yard goes around its two kept lines and the place in the strip where a hand went in and did not come out with a look and no number. A woman who asks what the reeds will cost is sent to the pot with *the house has said it*; Sella gives the instruction to the bucket, broom, pot and fire and never the basket, sets him on his bed under the roof and leaves the thread frozen, her hand resting a half-beat longer than the setting needed. On Reed 2 Jorra's grey dust comes out of the back room past the bent finger, his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and the boy reads the iron once more as the bite that holds and the bite that takes, an object worth nothing joined to no kiln; Tarn takes the broom only after its instruction, his seventh year pressed past the planting and still held, his stroke going on over the flagstones without the half-check while the stones keep the shallow bright patch where it stopped, neither man naming the other's silence. At the market the soft-cuffed clerk still sits for a measure nobody called and a foundling turns his head toward a pot half a beat after the boy's, after the knuckle and after the note, rubs a green sliver twice, opens his mouth on the tail of a sentence and lets nothing out that a clerk could take down — no loaf, no slate, no sale, the fifth delay held for the day it lands as the physical tell of a betrayal. On Reed 3 a reed-cutter at the Weir turns a length of wool or reed-fibre cord on his thumb, knots standing along it at arm's length with one tied low by teeth and thumb, and answers a woman who cannot read it with *You can ask the cord* and *No more can the Court*; **T099 is planted here**, a third private record in a material no Court thumb will read, carried and not counted, not laid beside the hand that did not come back, the six pages in the little tin, the pewter tin above the abandoned bed, Mira's second twist or Orra's knot-string. At the close the river's note comes through the finger's-width gap, the carried fire's four, four and a gap through the floor under blind ash, its payer unnamed for a hundred and twenty-two days, and the boards overhead keep four nails, four and a knot — three counts in three places that do not become one. Six hearings stay six beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, three intervals, the told bell beside the heard bell, the word forty unspoken, the cold foot still the cold to one woman and weather to the yard, one stiff crust two inches from his ear with its empty place unnamed, the shelf's two gaps in a room that does not touch it. Nobody says he is one hundred and twenty-two days old. His hand goes up an inch toward the note of water and comes down; nothing is joined, paid, named or ended.

On Days 123 through 125 he is one hundred and twenty-three, then one hundred and twenty-four, then one hundred and twenty-five days old — four months and three days through four months and five days — and nobody under any roof says so. Reed comes on without a boundary and the reeds go into the beds as the seed went into the strips: armfuls judged by the arm that carries them, a day's cutting a going-downriver and a coming-back at dusk, the cost asked in the form of a direction and answered with the weather or the river (T071/T072, T077, T058/T079). He reads the yard's going-around — the two unswept lines called the thaw, the space under the lowest rack, the place in the strip where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come back — as a chart closed with a neat line over a note nobody opened: exact, complete, worth nothing, and sets it on a place of its own, laying no likeness across the shelf's two gaps or the one crust in the fold (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried, T060/T086). On Day 124, while Jorra's finger stands in the cold damper and the dust from the back room is ground past it, Tarn's sentence about an ear worth more than a debt comes to the threshold of the word that would call the loan and stops there with no figure and no name; he files the stopped sentence on the interval tray as an object and not a rule, an interval and not a payment, and the loan stays uncalled and the seventh year held (T035/T031/T062, T036/T076, T061/T082). The damper's double bite is read once more as the iron that holds and takes, joined to no kiln, the two shut-room tellings kept beside it with no third telling (T065/T095). On Day 125 at the Weir he watches the reed-cutter straighten his grey cord on a thumb, tie one knot low with his teeth and put it back inside his coat with its knots unread, and holds it beside Mira's two cords, Orra's knot-string, six pages in a little tin and the pewter tin above the abandoned bed, none touching another, no tin or box opened (T099 carried, T059/T025/T024, T023/T089). Orra gives a date instead of a figure; Mira answers a thin run with her own answer and does not look into the basket; Berr wipes his thumb twice and reads one in twelve as the notice says; the soft-cuffed clerk sits for an uncalled measure and the market sells over him (T090/T080, T015/T041/T047). At the going of the light Sella sets him on his bed, puts the fold right without looking at his face, leaves the thread frozen, tells the fire it was the thaw and goes to her own bed; the door stands a finger's width open and the river's note, the fire's four-low-pulls-and-a-gap and the fifth that is a knot in the boards keep three counts in three places that do not become one (T088, T061/T082). The file is complete, exact and worthless, the near-saying at the threshold, the seventh year held, and the count goes on unspent into Day 126. Nothing was joined, paid, named or ended.

On Days 126 through 128 he is one hundred and twenty-six, then twenty-seven, then twenty-eight days old — four months and six days through four months and eight days — and nobody under any roof says so. The reeds stand in the yard ruts in rows at last, green at the broken ends and pale where the frost bit the tips, and the arm that carries a bundle is read as a record in muscle that tells what was carried and never what it cost; the yard goes around its two lines, the rack space and the place in the strip where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out with a look and a wide step and no number. The chapter's one movement is a repetition: the near-saying under the Vail roof comes a second time to the threshold of the word that would call the loan and stops again, and he files the second stopping beside the first on the interval tray as two intervals and not two payments, so that a kept thing gathers a second shape without becoming an account. At the market a third green at a mouth, a cup turned a quarter and set back, a pale cord-mark on the back of a hand, the soft-cuffed clerk with his slate for an uncalled measure and a girl taking the out-of-line landing sideways are each set on a place of their own; three likenesses in a row want to become a cause and he refuses the labour, having learned in two lives what a cause grows from. At the Weir the reed-cutter's cord, Orra's knot-string, Mira's two cords and the clerk's slate stand as four private records no Court thumb will spell, and he holds all four with one hand that cannot close two inches on the basket rim. Three doors down a mercy-lie curdles further against a death nobody can correct, six pages stay unread and both tins stay shut. At the going of the light the file comes back exact, complete and worthless: six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, two stoppings on the tray, one crust in the fold with its empty place unnamed, two gaps on a shelf in a room that does not touch, the word forty unspoken, the cold foot still the cold to one woman and weather to the yard, and three counts of river, fire and boards kept in three places that do not become one. One hundred and twenty-eight stands where one hundred and twenty-seven stood, exact, unspent and spoken by nobody; the hand goes up an inch toward the note of water over water and comes down. No power moved, no experiment, no recognition, no ending.

On Days 129 through 131 the file acquires a sixth private record and still refuses to become an account. The charcoal under the loaf shelf is the year's newest keeping: set where nobody expects a reader, seen from a low angle the house did not choose for him, and left unread beside the two gaps above it and the fold's crust in a room that does not touch. The near-saying has now stopped three times; three intervals on a tray are not a rule and are not a payment. The count reaches one hundred and thirty-one and is worth nothing to anybody who could enter it. Forward, not closed.

On Days 132 through 134 the private file acquires a fourth stopping and still refuses to become a doctrine. Four intervals lie on the tray as objects, a kept word brought to the teeth four times and not once said; four greens at mouths and five shapes stay a colour and not a name; the charcoal under the shelf, the reed-cutter's cord, Orra's knots, Mira's cords, Alda's six pages and both tins, and Dessa's pewter tin stay unread in materials no Court thumb can spell. The count reaches one hundred and thirty-four, exact, unspent and spoken by nobody, and the hand goes up an inch toward the river's note and comes down. Forward, not closed.

On Days 135 through 137 he is one hundred and thirty-five, then six, then seven days old — four months and fifteen, sixteen and seventeen days — and nobody under any roof says so. The reed month goes into its last third and he lives it one dated day at a time: armfuls judged by the arm that carried them, cost asked as a direction and answered with the weather and the river, the yard going around the clean dark line, the new finer grey called the thaw, the rack space with its broken birth-slates and the place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number. Under the Vail roof he watched Sella's finger go beneath the loaf shelf where the charcoal tally lies, without a look or a word, and made no cause of the habit; he read the carrying arm as a record in muscle that tells what was carried and not what it cost, exact, complete and worth nothing. On Day 136 Tarn brought the ear-sentence a fifth time to the word that would call the loan and stopped there without spending the breath, and the boy filed a fifth stopping on the interval tray beside four as an object and not a rule, an interval and not a payment, refusing to let five likenesses stand up and call themselves a doctrine, for a doctrine is an account that has found a payer. At the Weir and below the sill on Day 137 he kept the reed-cutter's grey cord, Orra's date with its readable unnamed forty-first double turn, Mira's second twist and the dark foot inside three turns of wool on places that do not touch, and read each exactly and worth nothing. At the going of the light he was set on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, the fold put right without a look at his face, the thread left frozen, the pot handle two inches beyond his reaching hand; in the small hours his file came back exact and complete and worthless, the river's note, the fire's missing fifth breath and the boards' fifth-that-was-a-knot still three counts in three places that did not become one, and his hand went up an inch toward the water and came down with one hundred and thirty-seven exact and unspent.

On Days 138 through 140 he is one hundred and thirty-eight, then thirty-nine, then forty days old --- four months and eighteen, nineteen and twenty days --- and nobody under any roof says so. The reed month stands in its last tend and the district's arithmetic stays in arms and weather: the last bundles come in judged by the arm that carried them, a woman at the racks who asks what the hands will be paid is answered with the weather and the river, and the yard goes around the two unswept lines, the rack space with its broken birth-slates and the place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number. Under the shelf Sella's finger goes beneath the board and comes away unread; at the Cross on the second Account-day Berr reads one in twelve and the true subtraction stays in the box, and a fifth green comes to a silent woman's mouth, a colour and not a name. At the hearth the sentence about an ear worth more than the debt that holds it comes a sixth time to the word that would call the loan and stops there with no figure and no name, the breath drawn and not spent; the boy files a sixth stopping on the interval tray beside five as an object and not a rule, an interval and not a payment, refusing to let six likenesses stand up and call themselves a doctrine, for a doctrine is an account that has found a payer. At the Weir the reed-cutter's cord, Orra's readable unnamed double turn and Mira's second twist stand within a dozen paces and none can be entered; below the sill the dark foot stays the cold to one woman and weather to the yard.
