In the small hours he turns out his file as a man lays instruments on a towel and finds every object in its place and nothing laid across anything else --- six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, six stoppings and no payer named to any, five greens as colours and not names, three counts of river, fire and boards in three places that do not become one --- and his hand goes up an inch toward the note of water over water and comes down with one hundred and forty exact and unspent.

On Days 141 through 143 he is one hundred and forty-one, then forty-two, then forty-three days old — four months and twenty-one through twenty-three days — and nobody under any roof says so. The reed month goes into its last week and the district's arithmetic stays in arms and weather: bundles are judged by the arm that carries them, a cost asked as a direction is answered with the river and the pot, and the yard goes around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates and the empty place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number. The chapter's one movement is a seventh likeness held apart by hand: Tarn brings the ear-sentence to the word that would call the loan on Reed 23 after Reed 5, 8, 11, 14, 17 and 20 and stops there with no figure and no name, drawing the breath he does not spend; the boy files the seventh stopping on the interval tray beside six as an interval and not a seventh payment, and refuses to let seven likenesses stand up and call themselves a doctrine, for a doctrine is an account that has found a payer and no payer has been named to any of the seven. A neighbour's one-breath naming of two absences at the loaf shelf advances T097 on a place of its own, unpaid, with no third gap and no finger laid. The charcoal under the shelf, the reed-cutter's cord, Orra's knots, Mira's second twist, Alda's six pages and both tins, Dessa's pewter tin, Berr's locked box and the clerk's inward slate go on being kept by hands that cannot read them, gathering toward a far-off hand that will lay them together and call the joining an account; the fifth green stays a colour, the cold foot stays uncured, and the loan stays uncalled with the seventh year one tend from its end. In the small hours he turns out his file complete, exact and worthless, finds every object in its place and nothing laid across anything else, wants the crust two inches from his ear and his number spoken by somebody who would know what a number was, and gets neither; his hand goes up an inch toward the note of water over water and comes down. Forward, not closed.

On Days 144 through 146 he is one hundred and forty-four, then forty-five, then forty-six days old — four months and twenty-four through twenty-six days — and nobody under any roof says so. The reed month goes out without a boundary drawn. The arm that carries the last bundles is read once more as a record in muscle that tells what was carried and never what it cost, exact, complete and worth nothing; a cost asked as a direction is answered with the weather and the river and leaves no entry. Under the Vail roof on Day 144 he watches Sella's finger go beneath the loaf shelf and come away without a look or a word — a hand that either knows the board by heart or has been told not to look, the reason left between the two — and reads nothing of the charcoal. On Day 145 Tarn brings the ear-sentence an eighth time, after Reed 5, 8, 11, 14, 17, 20 and 23, all the way to the word that would call the loan and stops there with no figure and no name, drawing the breath he does not spend; the boy files the eighth stopping on the interval tray beside seven as an interval and not a payment, an object among objects and not a rule, refusing to let eight likenesses stand up and call themselves a doctrine, for a doctrine is an account that has found a payer and no payer has been named to any of the eight. The loan stays uncalled and the seventh year stands one tend from its end and has not given. On Day 146 Alda covers the birth-flower with a dead woman's date and the mercy-lie curdles further against a death nobody can correct; her six pages and both tins, the pewter tin above the abandoned bed, the charcoal under the shelf, the reed-cutter's cord, Orra's knot-string, Mira's second twist and the soft-cuffed clerk's inward slate go on being kept by hands that cannot read them, gathering toward a far-off hand that will lay them together and call the joining an account. The count reaches one hundred and forty-six, exact, unspent and spoken by nobody; in the small hours his hand goes up an inch toward the river's note of water over water and comes down against the fold, wanting the crust two inches from his ear and his number said by somebody who would know what a number was, and getting neither. Forward, not closed.

On Days 147 through 149 the reed month goes out with the last bundles judged by the arm that carried them and the last market standing with every withholding in one square --- a figure read that is not the figure paid, a cord no Court thumb can read, a clerk set for a measure nobody called, a sixth green that is only a colour and a foundling's sixth half-turn that is only a shape. Under the Vail roof the near-saying comes to the teeth a **ninth** time and stops at the word that would call the loan, filed on the interval tray as an interval and not a payment; the boy holds nine likenesses apart by hand so they cannot stand up as a doctrine with a payer at its foot. The seventh year stands one tend from its end and has not given; the loan that can hear stays uncalled, and the count crosses into Smoke exact and unspent, spoken by nobody. His bond with Sella keeps its old shape --- instruction to the wool and the fire and never the basket, the half-beat longer at the setting, the fold put right without a look at his face --- and the foundling stays a shape in his file, not yet a speaker and not yet a betrayal.

**Ch 37.** The firing month opens while he is one hundred and fifty, then one hundred and fifty-one, then one hundred and fifty-two days old, told by nobody. At the kiln mouth he watches Sella touch the widow's mark before the opening for the first time in a hundred and fifty days, hold it one breath and let go, and tip the coal under a broken pot; the fire answers four low pulls and a silence whose payer is still unnamed. He reads the opening as a theatre he cannot enter --- heat kept and counted, a gate with two bites, a cost with no name under it --- and keeps the kiln's four-four-and-a-gap, the carried fire's under the floor and the river's note through the door on three places that do not become one. The pan set out to dry through Reed is gone with no scrape, crumb, print or word, and he holds its round empty place on a tray of its own, apart from the shelf's two gaps, the fold's one crust and its gap, the field's empty place, the sixth green and the sixth shape; no third gap comes and no fourth likeness is made a cause. He sees dry slate dust ground small as meal held apart from bone-coloured slip, a thing broken and a thing put together, and hears a yard woman speak sideways of a man in Mud End taken in his sleep. At the Weir the last reed work is a direction answered with a direction; five record-first hands --- midwife, thane, clerk, reed-cutter and Sella's finger beneath the shelf --- perform one grammar that no one explains, and a cost asked of a firing is sent to a pot that never answers. His file comes back exact, complete and worth nothing on all three nights: six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, nine stoppings of the near-saying as objects and not a rule, no tenth stood up before the seventh year is earned, the damper's double bite joined to no kiln, the two shut-room tellings with no third, the told bell beside the heard. On Day 152 the ear-sentence rises as it had nine times before, but this time it stops short of the threshold and no tenth stopping stands; the loan stays uncalled and the Seed 16 breath unspent. The old life stays offstage: no present-tense break, no tagged memory, no departure from his eyes. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 38.** Through Days 153–159 he is one hundred and fifty-three, then one hundred and fifty-four, one hundred and fifty-five, one hundred and fifty-six, one hundred and fifty-seven, one hundred and fifty-eight and one hundred and fifty-nine days old — five months and three days to five months and nine days — and nobody under any roof says so. The firing month settles into its working week: the kiln breathes behind the wall in fours with a gap, the carried fire answers four low pulls with the fifth missing under the floor, and the river keeps its note through the finger's-width door, three counts in three places that do not become one. He reads an opening as a theatre he cannot enter and the damper as one iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, joined to no kiln. The yard goes around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack and the place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number. Downriver the last reed work has become a direction counted in the carrying arm, which knows the weight and not the cost; Orra gives a date where a figure is asked, Mira shows the second twist twice with neither knot counted, and the reed-cutter ties one knot low with his teeth and puts his cord away unread. The surgeon's knowledge of the cold foot bought nothing. On Day 157 Sella's finger goes beneath the loaf shelf's front edge where the charcoal lies, without looking, and he sees the hand go under and come back and nothing of what the marks say. A woman raking the kiln mouth speaks sideways of a man in Mud End taken quiet in his sleep and called merciful; nobody makes a lesson of it and nobody puts a figure to him. On the eve of the Account-day the first new shape of the month arrives: Hessa Rale of Slate Row, a runner carrying Berr's narrow slate edge-on between the Cross and Greybank, her hand to the slate before her eyes to the yard. The boy files that order of reaching as an eighth record-first hand on a place of its own — not Marra, not the neat dark hand, not the Court-table clerk, not the soft-cuffed clerk, not the soft hands at Slate Row, not Berr's twice-wiped thumb, not the finger under the shelf — and does not let eight likenesses stand up and call themselves a rule. On Day 159 Berr reads one in twelve at the Cross, denies the levy with *your house*, never *I*, and goes off with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, believing he has done a kindness; the heard bell stands beside the told bell, four, four and a gap. In the small hours his file is exact and worthless: six hearings with no seventh, nine stoppings with no payer and no tenth, one crust and its empty place, two shelf gaps, the pan's gone place, a sixth colour and a sixth shape on places of their own, the loan uncalled and the Seed 16 breath unspent. One hundred and fifty-nine stands exact, complete and unspent; the fire misses its fifth and the charcoal waits for a hand that is not his and a day that has not come. The old life stayed offstage: no remembered line, no present-tense break, no departure from his eyes. Nothing joined, named, paid or ended.

**Ch 39.** Through Days 160–162 he is one hundred and sixty, then one hundred and sixty-one, then one hundred and sixty-two days old — five months and ten days to five months and twelve days — and nobody under any roof says so. The firing month’s second working week goes by with the kiln breathing in fours behind the wall, the carried fire missing its fifth under the floor with its payer unnamed for a hundred and sixty, a hundred and sixty-one and a hundred and sixty-two days, and the river keeping its note through the finger’s-width door: three counts in three places that do not become one. He reads the firing as an operating theatre he cannot enter — heat kept and counted behind a screen, a gate with two bites, a cost paid by a man whose name is not spoken — and the carrying arm as a record in muscle that keeps weight and not cost. On Day 160 Sella’s finger goes beneath the loaf shelf’s front edge where the charcoal lies, without looking and without a word; the shelf keeps its two gaps, the fold its one crust and its gap, the wall its gone pan, and no third gap or fourth likeness comes. On Day 161, for the first time under this roof, Jorra’s bent finger goes to the cold damper and stops until Sella has finished *It wants feeding*, then moves the tongue a hair’s breadth and leaves it standing; the boy files the fraction on a place of its own as a change held and unspoken, not a reconciliation, not a hearing, not a payment, no payer named, kept apart from Tarn’s unchecking stroke and from the nine stoppings that remain nine objects with no tenth. Jorra’s grey dust, his *You’ll do* and his knowing withheld from a High Kiln kiln-master go on gathering interest; the kiln-master says a pot that sings early generally sings itself out early and no pot is lost in the yard. On Day 162, downriver at the Weir, the finished reeds have become a direction counted in the arm that carries them; Orra gives a date where a figure is asked, Mira answers a thin run with *Ask the river*, the reed-cutter hides his cord, the soft-cuffed clerk stays set for a measure nobody called, and a child’s foot stays dark in three turns of wool while the surgeon’s reading of the cold buys nothing at two paces. At the going of the light Sella carries him home around the pan’s gone place, the trough and the rack space, sets him on his bed, tells the fire it was the thaw and *The house will keep it*, and sets the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand. In the small hours the file is exact and worthless: six hearings with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, nine stoppings with no payer; one hundred and sixty-two stands exact, complete and unspent, the fire misses its fifth, and the charcoal waits for a hand that is not his and a day that has not come.

Through Days 163–169 the boy is one hundred and sixty-three to one hundred and sixty-nine days old, told by nobody, and the firing month's third working week goes past with the kiln brought up and nothing paid. He reads the firing as an operating theatre he cannot enter --- heat kept and counted behind a screen, a gate with two bites, a cost paid by a man whose name is not spoken --- and he files the district's arithmetic in separate places so no account can stand up out of it: the river's fifth low note, the fire's missing fifth and the boards' four nails, four and a knot are three counts in three places that do not become one. On Day 166 a hand beneath the loaf shelf stays a beat longer than it used to and the meaning stays exactly where it was; on Day 169 the second Account-day reads a figure over a basket that cannot hold a figure, a woman carries a second figure up the road edge-on and will not read it, and the count reaches one hundred and sixty-nine exact, complete and unspent. He has stopped expecting to be told what the number counts, which is not the same as not wanting it.

**Ch 41.** Through Days 170–172 he is one hundred and seventy, then one hundred and seventy-one, then one hundred and seventy-two days old — five months and twenty days to five months and twenty-two days — and nobody under any roof says so. The last working week keeps the kilns at their heat; the danger gathers in dust ground small as meal, the sweetish lead edge on a wrong wind and the pot that gives its note before its time, and no room is changed, no pot lost and no lesson made. He reads the firing as an operating theatre he cannot enter — heat kept and counted behind a screen, a gate with two bites, a cost paid by a man whose name is not spoken — and the carrying arm as a record in muscle that keeps weight and not cost. The kiln's breath behind the wall, the carried fire's missing fifth under the floor and the river's note through the finger's-width door stay three counts in three places, and overhead the boards keep four nails, four and a knot the width of a thumb. On Day 171, while Jorra's bent finger waited in the cold damper and Sella's unfinished sentence was allowed to finish, Tarn's broom checked half a beat behind the standing finger on the flagstones and went on without a word or a turn of the head — the Seed 22 half-check returned changed after a hundred and fifty days. The boy filed it on a place of its own beside the Day 161 fraction and the Day 166 held beat: three held changes on three separate places, not a pair, not a doctrine and not a rule, because a rule is an account that has found a payer and no payer stands to any of the three. The held beat beneath the shelf was not repeated and the charcoal waited unread. Six hearings stayed six with no seventh and none invented, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, nine stoppings objects with no tenth before the seventh year is earned, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled. In the small hours he laid the file out as a man lays instruments on a towel and found it exact and worthless: he could want the crust two inches from his ear, the pot handle standing beyond his hand and a number spoken by somebody who would have known what a number counted, and could reach none of them. He had stopped expecting to be told what the number counts, which is not the same as not wanting it. One hundred and seventy-two stood exact, complete and unspent, spoken by nobody; the payer under the floor had had no name for a hundred and seventy-two days. The old life stayed offstage: no remembered line, no present-tense break and no departure from his eyes. Forward, not closed.
**Ch 49 (bonds).** Through Days 198–200 nothing changed hands under the Vail roof and nobody said what the number counted. Sella's instruction went to the wool, fire, pot, broom and running water and never the basket; she said *the house*, kept her half-pace and her jar-glance without a touch, set the pot handle two inches beyond his hand with the half-beat at setting, fed him from the house's own dish with his mouth unwiped, and left the mark where it hung, because a market and a reading are not openings and there has been no opening since Ember 1. On Day 199 she carried him to the Cross and stood half a pace off in the moot-post's shade through Berr's second reading — *one in twelve, as the notice says*, said twice over the basket, and *your house*, never *I* — while the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself; the box went up the locked road with the true subtraction inside and the printed figure standing, and the reading answered nothing for the man carried out on Day 188. With Tarn the equality held at the place it had moved to — six counted and the seventh held, the ear-sentence at the word that would call the loan and stopped, nine stoppings with no tenth, the breath unspent — and the boy did not join his own held inch to the held breath. With Jorra the dust, the withheld note and the remembered finger at the damper stood as before; *he's not Vail* sealed and the promise ungiven. Hessa's slate went up unread, the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set for his uncalled measure, the green stayed a colour and Ulda a shape. No name given, no tin or box opened, no collector called, no payer named; the old life stayed offstage.

**Ch 50 (bonds).** Through Days 201–203 nothing changed hands under the Vail roof and nobody said what the number counted. Sella's instruction went to the wool, fire, pot, broom, running water and river wind and never the basket; she said *the house*, kept her half-pace and her jar-glance without a touch, set the pot handle two inches beyond his hand with the half-beat at setting, fed him from the house's own dish with his mouth unwiped, moved her lips along a row with two unnamed gaps and gave nobody a figure, kept her finger from beneath the charcoal, and left the mark where it hung, because a morning and a shelf are not openings and there has been no opening since Ember 1. Cost-questions went to the pot and the weather on all three days and went away level. With Tarn the equality held at the lip of the sill — six counted and the seventh held, the ear-sentence brought all the way to the word that would call the loan and stopped, nine stoppings with no tenth, the Seed 16 breath unspent, no collector — and the boy did not join his own held inch to the held breath. With Jorra the dust, the withheld note and the remembered finger at the damper stood as before; *he's not Vail* sealed and the promise ungiven. Hessa's slate lay unread at Greybank, the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set for his uncalled measure, the green stayed a colour and Ulda a shape. **The inch, advanced and not paid:** in the small hours of Day 203 he asked his hand again; it went up an inch toward the river's note, stayed up through the whole of one of the breaths Sella counts at her fire in the room that does not touch his, did not come down with her breath, kept the height through the silence after her count had ended and came down on the river's next low note — a coordination with a second rhythm nobody counts and never gave him, filed on a sixth place, not a hearing, seventh, stopping, payment, key, power or rule, no payer named. No name given, no tin or box opened, no collector called, no payer named; the old life stayed offstage.

On Days 173 through 179, the firing month's last tend and its third Account-day, he is one hundred and seventy-three through one hundred and seventy-nine days old and nobody says so. The kilns stand at their last heat, the dust is ground small as meal, the sweetish lead edge rides a wrong wind and a pot sings before its time; no room is changed and no pot is lost. He reads the firing as a theatre he cannot enter, heat counted behind a screen and a gate of two bites with a cost paid by a man whose name is not spoken. On the last market-day Sella sets the basket in the moot-post's shade where no slate lies while Berr reads one in twelve for the third time, denies the levy with *your house* and goes off up the clay road with the box locked. The file laid out in the small hours is exact and worthless: six hearings beside and never among, three near-full answers, nine stoppings with no tenth, three held changes on three places. One hundred and seventy-nine stands complete and unspent. He wants a number spoken by someone who would have known what a number counted and gets none; the month goes out without a boundary drawn.

Through Days 180–182 he is one hundred and eighty, then one hundred and eighty-one, then one hundred and eighty-two days old — six months exactly on the first morning and six months and two days at the close — and nobody under any roof says so. Ember comes on without a boundary drawn in the narration, the district still calling Tallow the firing month and Ember the kiln month, neither usage corrected. The first smell is hot clay under tallow and wet wool; the first sound is the kiln's breath in fours with a gap, not a bell. Under the floor the carried fire answers four low pulls and the silence where the fifth should have been, its payer unnamed for a hundred and eighty, a hundred and eighty-one and a hundred and eighty-two days; through the finger's-width door the river keeps its note of water over water, a fifth low thing never counted among the hearings; overhead the boards keep four nails, four, and a knot the width of a thumb. Three counts in three places, and none becomes the others. **The chapter's one meaningful change** is the first firing of Ember on the morning of Day 180: Sella's hand goes to the widow's mark before the kiln mouth, holds one breath, lets go; the clay lid comes up; the carried coal goes under the back of a broken pot; the fire takes, answering four with its fifth still missing and no payer. He reads it in the surgeon's register as an opening performed on a vessel by a hand that will not name what it mends, heat held behind a screen and let through a gate of two bites; nobody praises it and nobody sees it broken. The days after are ordinary and the month's danger keeps an order with no name that cannot be carried into a chart: hot clay, slate dust ground small as meal under the nails and white in a knuckle when a fist opens, the sweetish lead edge of glaze on a wrong wind, and a pot set down wrong giving its high thin note before its time, which the woman who set it down does not hear. He turns toward the note and gets no further. A neighbour asking a cost is sent to the pot and the weather and goes away level, nothing priced. Men unnamed go into the back room before first grey on the last two mornings and get no name from the house; Jorra's knowing of a pot's full note goes to the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid, and no pot is lost. In the small hours he lays the file out as instruments on a towel: six hearings beside and never among with the sixth from a person and no seventh invented, three near-full answers advanced and unpaid, nine stoppings as objects and not a rule with no tenth before the seventh year is earned, the Day 161 damper fraction, the Day 166 shelf beat and the Day 171 flagstone half-check on three separate places and not stood up as a doctrine, two shut-room tellings with no third, the told bell beside the heard. One hundred and eighty-two stands exact, complete and unspent in the dark of the wool. He wants the crust, the breast, the pot handle two inches beyond his hand and a number spoken by somebody who would have known what a number counted, and gets none; he has stopped expecting to be told, which is not the same as not wanting it. He is the only adult under the roof and the least use in it. Old life offstage: no remembered line, no present-tense break, no departure from his eyes.

Through Days 183–185 (Ember 4–6) he is one hundred and eighty-three, then one hundred and eighty-four, then one hundred and eighty-five days old — six months and three days to six months and five days — and nobody under any roof says so. The kiln month's second working week settles into the same arithmetic: the first smell hot clay under tallow and wet wool with the sour mineral breath of the unswept corner, the first sound the kiln's breath in fours with a gap, not a bell; under the floor four low pulls and the silence where the fifth should have been, its payer unnamed for a hundred and eighty-three, four and five days; through the finger's-width door the river's note of water over water, a fifth low thing never counted among the hearings; overhead four nails, four, and a knot the width of a thumb — three counts in three places, and none becomes the others. He reads the week's ordinary tending as a theatre kept behind a screen and a gate of two bites, the carrying arm as a record in muscle that keeps weight and not cost, and the surgeon's register turns the private file out as instruments laid on a towel, exact, complete and worth nothing at two paces. The week's one meaningful change happens above and behind him: on the afternoon of Day 183 Tarn lifts the warm stone out of its pot in the Debt Loft, sets it on the sill where the light comes through the finger's-width opening, looks at it a long moment and puts it back; no word, no figure, no payer and no forgiveness. The boy hears only the small dry scrape of a pot's foot on stone from a room he cannot enter, files it on a place of its own and refuses to let a fourth likeness stand up and call itself a cause, for a rule is an account that has found a payer. On the later days he files the remembering as a remembering and not a holding made again. The danger keeps an order and no name — fine grey slate dust ground small as meal and white in a knuckle when a fist opens, the sweetish lead edge on a wrong wind, a pot set down wrong giving its note before its time which the woman who set it down does not hear — and he files the early note as a danger of the month, never as a fourth near-full answer or a hearing; no pot is lost and no lesson made. Sella's instruction goes to wool, fire, pot, broom and running water and never the basket; *the house*; the half-pace; the clay jar glanced at cold under its glaze without a touch; the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand with the half-beat at setting; the widow's mark left where it hung, because the hand goes to the mark before the kiln and at no other hour. He goes wide around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the rack space with its broken birth-slates, the pan's gone ring and the field's empty place where a hand went in on Seed 15, with a look and no number. In the small hours the file stays exact and worthless: six hearings beside and never among with the sixth from a person and no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, nine stoppings as objects with no tenth before the seventh year is earned, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled, three held changes on three separate places and the loft-sill sound on a fourth, the told bell beside the heard, the charcoal unread, the crust's empty place unnamed, the two shelf gaps, the pan's gone ring and the field's empty place on trays that do not touch. He wants the crust, the breast, the pot handle standing two inches beyond his hand and a number spoken by somebody who would have known what a number counted, and gets none; he has stopped expecting to be told, which is not the same as not wanting it. One hundred and eighty-five stands exact, complete and unspent in the dark of the wool, and the count goes forward into Day 186. Old life offstage: no remembered line, no present-tense break, no departure from his eyes.

Through Days 186–188 (Ember 7–9) he is one hundred and eighty-six, then one hundred and eighty-seven, then one hundred and eighty-eight days old — six months and six days to six months and eight days — and nobody under any roof says so. Three dated days are lived in full; the kiln breathes in fours with a gap behind the wall, the carried fire answers four low pulls with the fifth missing under the floor and its payer unnamed for 186, 187 and 188 days, the river's note comes through the finger's-width door and the boards keep four nails, four and a knot the width of a thumb, three counts in three places that do not become one (T088, T071/T072). The surgeon's register reads the household as a theatre kept behind a screen, the carrying arm as a record in muscle that keeps weight and not cost, and the private file as instruments laid on a towel. Then the gathered danger of the month is paid in one small physical edge, entered nowhere: a rack-worker's pot gives its high thin note before its time and sings itself out early with no lesson made, and a glaze-room hand is carried out of the kiln and across the yard toward the clay road, alive and breathing, the sweet-sickness on his breath, the pale line at his gum, his hand held limp with fingers the colour of old rope and pewter-pale nails, one carrier's thumb hooked under his arm to keep the head from lopping. The boy reads the body exactly as a man with lead in him — the only adult in the yard and the least use in it — and cannot reach, speak or spend any of it; he files the pale line on a place of its own, not a hearing, not a seventh, not a fourth near-full answer, not a stopping, not one of the three held changes and not a fourth likeness stood up to call itself a cause, for a rule is an account that has found a payer (T077 advanced, not paid; T061/T082 advanced). No major character dies, no collector is called, no figure is given, no slate is opened and no payer is named; the cost stays entered nowhere and the man goes on breathing up the road. He does not name lead-sickness in Grathic and joins the body to none of the absences; the High Kiln saying about a pot singing early is not made true as a lesson. Instruction to wool, fire, pot, broom and running water, never the basket; *the house*; half-pace; widow's mark untouched; jar glanced at without a touch; pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand; two rooms that do not touch (T058/T079). The yard goes around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the rack space with its broken birth-slates, the pan's gone ring, the field's empty place and the place the carriers crossed, with a look and a wide step and no number (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried). The nine stoppings stay nine objects with no tenth before the seventh year is earned, the Seed 16 breath is unspent and the loan uncalled; six hearings stay six beside and never among with the sixth from a person and no seventh, three near-full answers stand advanced and unpaid, three held changes stand on three places with the stone's loft-sill scrape on a fourth, and the two shut-room tellings stay two with no third. The leathery crust lies two inches from his ear with its empty place unnamed and no third, the thread of drool stays frozen and unwiped, cold comes to cheek, temple and the bare wrist joint and stops as if at the end of its own order, and the afterdrop waits past a warming nobody will perform. No power moved, no rank changed, no experiment, no money, no Chicago break and no tagged memory. The count stands one hundred and eighty-eight, exact, complete and unspent.

On Days 189 through 191, Ember 10–12, the first Account-day and market-day of the kiln month is read over the basket and the district's arithmetic changes for the month and cannot be undone: Berr reads *one in twelve, as the notice says*, says *The levy is called. It is not called by me* and *your house*, never *I*, and goes off with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, believing he has done a kindness, while the printed figure stands unchallenged and a figure read aloud is still not the figure paid. Hessa takes the slate up the road unread. The reading does not answer the man carried out on Day 188: no figure, collector or slate knows what he cost, and the pale line lies on a place of its own. He is one hundred and ninety-one days old, six months and eleven days, and nobody says so; the count stands exact, complete and unspent. The old life stays offstage. Forward, not closed.

Through Days 192 through 194, Ember 13–15, Thirdday to Fifthday, the account has gone away up the road in Berr's locked box and nothing under the Vail roof changes hands. He is one hundred and ninety-two, then ninety-three, then ninety-four days old — six months and twelve days to six months and fourteen days — and nobody under any roof says so; he has stopped expecting to be told what a number counts, which is not the same as not wanting it. Three dated days are lived in full, two scenes each; the kiln breathes in fours with a gap behind the wall, the carried fire answers four low pulls with the silence where the fifth should have been under the floor, its payer unnamed for a hundred and ninety-two, ninety-three and ninety-four days, the river keeps its note of water over water through the finger's-width door, and the boards keep four nails, four, and a knot the width of a thumb — three counts in three places that do not become one. Instruction goes to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom and the running water and never the basket; *the house*; the half-pace; the jar-glance without a touch; the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand with the half-beat at setting; gruel from the house's own dish with his mouth unwiped; the mark untouched because a morning and a shelf are not openings and there has been no opening since Ember 1; the two rooms that do not touch. A neighbour's cost-question goes to the pot and the weather on all three days and arrives nowhere (T077). The yard goes around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the rack space with its broken birth-slates, the pan's gone round ring, the bare dark trough, the field's empty place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and the place the carriers crossed, with a look and a wide step and no number (T009/T013/T092/T098). The sweet edge on a wrong wind is taken as a direction and the pot that sang early is swept around, not made an omen or a lesson; no collector is called for the man carried out on Day 188, no figure given, no sickness named in Grathic and no slate opened, and his pale line stays on a fifth place across nothing (T064 held). Tarn brings the ear-sentence to the threshold of the word that would call the loan and stops there on all three days, draws the breath he does not spend, keeps six counted where the house can check and the seventh at the lip where it cannot, nine stoppings with no tenth before the seventh year is earned, and sweeps the collector's road without turning (T036/T076). Jorra's dust comes out ground small as meal from men he does not name, the full note known three turns ahead and withheld from a man on the wrong wind, the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, and his bent finger standing in the cold damper as a remembered repetition, not a new change (T035/T031/T062/T065/T095). The charcoal stays unread beneath the shelf and Sella's finger does not go beneath its front edge (T100); the warm stone stays cold and unreconciled in its unmarked pot in the loft (T006). **The chapter's one meaningful change** is a fraction of the body, not a breakthrough: on the afternoon of Day 193 his hand outside the wool goes up an inch toward the note of water over water and hesitates for the length of the small pause in the kiln's breath before coming down; on the morning of Day 194 it goes up and comes down at once; and in the small hours it goes up, holds for the length of one of the breaths Sella counts at her fire in the room that does not touch his — the small intake, the hold, the letting go — and then comes down against the fold. He files the held inch on a place of its own beside the others and forbids it to stand up: not a hearing, not a seventh, not a stopping, not a payment, not one of the three held changes, not a key and not a rule, with no payer named and no doctrine stood up out of it. The file comes back exact, complete and worthless — six hearings beside and never among with the sixth out of a person and no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and unpaid, nine stoppings as objects with no tenth, three held changes on three places, the loft scrape on a fourth and the pale line on a fifth, the crust's gap, two shelf gaps, the pan's ring and the field's empty place on trays that do not touch — and he still cannot close the two inches to the pot handle, lift his chin to the leathery crust or speak a number. The body humiliates him and moves by an inch at the same time, and both are true. Cold stops at cheek, temple and the small bare joint at the wrist where the wool rides back, and the afterdrop waits on the far side of a warming nobody in this house will perform. No power moved, no rank changed, no experiment, no money, no Chicago break and no tagged memory. One hundred and ninety-four stands exact, complete and unspent in the dark of the wool, spoken by nobody under any roof, and the count goes forward into Day 195. Forward, not closed.

Through Days 195–197 (Ember 16–18) he is one hundred and ninety-five, then ninety-six, then ninety-seven days old — six months and fifteen days to six months and seventeen days — and nobody under any roof says so; he has stopped expecting to be told what a number counts, which is not the same as not wanting it. The kiln month's second working week runs on without a boundary drawn and the district keeps its two names for the months uncorrected. The file is laid out as instruments on a towel and found exact, complete and worthless: six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and unpaid, nine stoppings as objects with no tenth, three held changes on three places, the loft scrape on a fourth, the pale line on a fifth. **The chapter's one meaningful change is the inch asked again three times in three days and held three ways** — Day 195 for one counted breath, Day 196 down before the breath was done, Day 197 morning down at once, and in the small hours of Day 197 up through the whole intake and hold and on into the letting go, down with the breath rather than before it, the longest holding and a coordination, not a reach. It is filed on a sixth place of its own and never laid across the others: not a hearing, not a seventh, not a stopping, not a payment, not a key, not a power and not a rule, with no payer named and no doctrine stood up out of it. The body humiliates him and moves by an inch at the same time; both are true, and the inch is worth nothing because he still cannot close two inches, lift the crust or speak a number. The old life stays offstage; forward, not closed.

Through Days 198–203 (Ember 19–24) the kiln month's last working weeks run on without a boundary drawn and the count crosses two hundred exact and unspent, two hundred and three at the close, six months and twenty-three days, and nobody under any roof says so. On Day 199 Ember's second Account-day is read over the basket: Berr reads *One in twelve, as the notice says* twice, once for the moot and once for those who came late, says *The levy is called. It is not called by me* and *your house*, never *I*, and goes off with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, believing he has done a kindness; the printed figure stands unchallenged, a figure read twice is still not the figure paid, and the reading answers nothing for the man carried out on Day 188. The last tend opens on Day 201 and runs into its third day with no closing drawn: the carried fire misses its fifth for two hundred and one, two and three days with no payer named, and the river's note, the fire's gap and the boards' four nails, four and a thumb-width knot stay three counts in three places that do not become one, for agreeing them would want a payer and none stands to any. The surgeon's register reads a cost asked as a direction answered with the pot and the weather, the carrying arm as a record in muscle that knows weight and not cost, and the private file as instruments laid on a towel, exact, complete and worth nothing. **Through the three days he asks his hand five ways, and on Day 203 in the small hours it answers with a rhythm nobody counts:** up an inch toward the river's note, held through a whole breath Sella counts at her fire in the room that does not touch his, not down with her breath, held through the silence after her count, and down on the river's next low note — a coordination with a second rhythm never given to him, filed on a sixth place and never promoted into a hearing, a seventh, a stopping, a payment, a key, a power or a rule, no payer named. He still cannot close the two inches to the pot handle, lift the crust or speak a number; the body humiliates him and moves by an inch in the same motion, and both are true. The old life stays offstage. Forward, not closed.

Through Days 204–206 (Ember 25–27) the kiln month's last working week runs on under the Vail roof with no boundary drawn and no lesson made. He is two hundred and four, then five, then six days old — six months and twenty-four days to six months and twenty-six days — and nobody under any roof says so; he has stopped expecting to be told what a number counts, which is not the same as not wanting it. The carried fire misses its fifth for the same three days with no payer, and the river's note, the fire's gap and the boards' four nails, four and a thumb-width knot stay three counts in three places that do not become one, for agreeing them would want a payer and none stands to any. The house keeps its dead man in objects: the salt-stiff coat holding a broader man's shape, the seat hollow with a twenty-year leftward lean, the clean dark line and the finer grey called the thaw gone around with a look and a wide step and not swept, the proverb gone to the pot, the debt against the tiles neither paid nor settled nor stopped, and no collector called. The file laid out as instruments on a towel is exact, complete and worth nothing at two paces. **Two advancements, both unpaid:** a word comes up from Slate Row, spoken over the boards and not to the basket, that a slate was burned in the night — no owner, no name, no cause, filed on a place of its own beside Jorra's dust and the two shut-room tellings with a hand between them, a direction arriving nowhere and nothing a clerk could carry into a chart (T035/T031/T065/T095 advanced, not paid); and in the small hours of Day 206, with no count at Sella's fire to hold against, the hand goes up an inch toward the river's note and stays for the length of the river's own low interval, coming down on the river's next low note — a coordination with the one rhythm through the door, on its sixth place, no hearing, seventh, stopping, payment, key, power or rule and no payer named (T061/T082 advanced, not paid). Six answers in three days, each on its own tray; he still cannot close two inches, lift the crust or speak a number. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 52.** The kiln month's last working week is lived one dated day at a time on Days 207–209, Ember 28–30, and the arc's great subject becomes a third reading held beside two earlier ones and a hand refused under the very conditions that once answered. The boy is two hundred and seven, then eight, then nine days old — six months and twenty-seven through twenty-nine days — and nobody under any roof says so; the carried fire's fifth has had no payer for the same three days, and the river's note, the fire's gap and the boards' four nails, four and a thumb-width knot keep three counts in three places that do not become one, because agreeing them would want a payer and none stands to any. The household keeps Garl in objects to the very last day — coat, hollow seat, two unswept lines called the thaw, proverb gone to the pot, the uncalled debt against the tiles — without lesson or collector, his name unspoken for two hundred and nine days. Sella gives instruction to everything but the basket, keeps her half-pace and her two inches, moves her lips along a row with two gaps and gives nobody a figure, keeps her finger from beneath the charcoal and leaves the mark untouched because twenty-nine days have brought no opening. On Day 209 the district's arithmetic changes for the third time in the kiln month and cannot be changed back: Berr reads *One in twelve, as the notice says* twice over the basket, takes himself out of his own sentence with *your house*, never *I*, and goes off toward High Kiln with the box locked, believing he has done a kindness, while Hessa carries the slate up the road unread and the soft-cuffed clerk stays set at the landing for a measure nobody called. The bell goes four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself; the sixth green stays a colour and the foundling a shape with no seventh of either. The burned-slate word comes a second time and arrives nowhere, and the boy files it beside Jorra's dust and the two shut-room tellings with a hand between the three. In the small hours of the last day, with no breathing number anywhere in the house, the hand goes up an inch toward the river's note and comes down at once against the fold — a miss under the conditions that gave the Day 206 holding, filed on its sixth place, exact, complete and worth nothing. Nothing is joined, named, opened, called, spent or ended; Ember goes out without a boundary drawn and the count goes forward into Grain unspent. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 53.** Ember gives way to Grain without a boundary drawn and neither of the district's two month-names is corrected, and the firing thins into the ordinary tending of a year begun. The boy is two hundred and ten, eleven and twelve days old — six months and thirty to thirty-two days — and nobody under any roof says so; the carried fire's fifth has had no payer for the same three days; fire, river and boards keep three counts in three places that do not become one, for agreeing them would want a payer and none stands to any. The household's keeping passes from Ember's last row into Grain's first with no figure spoken: the uncalled debt against the tiles is another tend older, Tarn's seventh year stands at its very end and still has not given, and Berr's box with Ember's third true subtraction is up the clay road and cannot be reached back. The yard goes around its absences with a look and a wide step and no number, and the hand is asked six times across the three days and answered as the body answers, the sixth in the small hours held through a whole counted breath at Sella's fire in the room that does not touch his — the old two-rhythm coordination remembered, filed on a sixth place beside the others and laid across nothing, not a longer reach, a hearing, a seventh, a stopping, a payment, a key, a power or a rule. The file is exact, complete and worth nothing; the count goes forward unspent into Day 213, Grain 4, Fourthday, with Grain 10 (Day 219) held as the first Account-day where the seventh green and the seventh sighting are owed and must not come early. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 54.** The first working week of Grain is lived one dated day at a time on Days 213–215, Grain 4–6, Fourthday to Sixthday, three days in full and six scenes, with no market and no Account-day until Grain 10 (Day 219). The boy is two hundred and thirteen, fourteen and fifteen days old — six months and thirty-three days to six months and thirty-five days — and nobody under any roof says so; the carried fire answers four low pulls with the silence where the fifth should have been, its payer unnamed for all three days, while the river keeps water over water through the finger's-width door and the boards keep four nails, four and a thumb-width knot: three counts in three places that do not become one, for agreeing them would want a payer and none stands to any (T071/T072, T088, T061/T082). The file laid out as instruments on a towel is exact, complete and worth nothing: six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and unpaid, told bell beside heard, nine stoppings with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, three held changes on three places with the loft scrape fourth, the pale line fifth and the held inch sixth, and the burned-slate word on its own place beside Jorra's dust and the two shut-room tellings with a hand between the three. **The chapter's one meaningful change, gathered and not paid:** a kiln-hand come up on a wrong wind says the pot that sang before its time last month has not been heard from since; the saying turns by a hair and the boy takes it apart as a surgeon takes a wound apart — a direction with no hand at the foot of it, no owner, no room, no cause and no figure — and sets the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them (T061/T082 advanced, T064, T035/T031). No pot is lost, no name is given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188, and the sweet edge of lead glaze goes out on the wind that brought it. The burned-slate word comes over the boards a third time from Slate Row on Day 215 and arrives nowhere, still not a third telling of the shut counting room, which stays at two (T035/T031/T065/T095 advanced, not paid). The hand is asked as the days ask: down at once, held for the small intake, down before the counted breath, kept to the river's low interval with no count in the house, down at once under conditions that have sometimes answered, and in the small hours of Day 215 held through the small intake and the hold of one of the breaths Sella counts at her fire in the room that does not touch his, coming down on the letting go — a coordination shorter than the night before, on its sixth place and laid across nothing, no payer named (T061/T082). He still cannot close the two inches to the pot handle, lift the leathery crust or speak a number; the count goes forward exact and unspent, and the last fraction is held and let go, not spent and not explained. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 55.** Grain's second working week is lived one dated day at a time on Days 216–218 with no market and no Account-day until Grain 10, the count going into two hundred and eighteen exact, complete and unspent and spoken by nobody while the carried fire misses its fifth with its payer unnamed for the same three days. The chapter gathers rather than resolves: a pot saying takes a third shaping on the near-full tray — sung early, not heard from since, gone quiet on every row come the thaw — with three likenesses held apart and no payer named to any, and the burned-slate word comes a fourth time over the boards from Slate Row, a direction arrived nowhere and still not a third telling of the counting room shut in the war. Tarn's ninth stopping stands with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled; Jorra's quiet deepens and the promise stays ungiven; the mark stays untouched, the charcoal unread, the stone cold and Garl in objects; the green stays a colour and the foundling a shape with no seventh of either, owed at the market on Day 219. The hand is asked six ways, the last a two-rhythm coordination held through one of Sella's breaths in the room that does not touch his and down on the river's next low note — remembered, worth nothing, never a power or a sign. Forward, not closed.

Through Grain 10–12 he lives Grain's first Account-day and market and the two tending days after it without anything being joined. The count goes forward exact at two hundred and nineteen, twenty and twenty-one, the fire keeps four, four and a gap with no payer, and the market offers its withholdings in one square: a figure read twice that is not the figure paid, a slate carried up unread, a clerk set for an uncalled measure, a bell struck four, four and a gap, a seventh green that is only a colour and a seventh shape that is only a shape. He takes the two new things apart as a surgeon takes apart a wound and sets them on separate places, with no name, no ear, no cause and no joining; the loves that cannot be booked stay unbooked and the sale years ahead. His hand answers six times across the three days, the last held through the intake, hold and letting go of one of Sella's breaths and down on the river's next low note, a two-rhythm coordination remembered and worth nothing. The file is exact, complete and worth nothing; he still cannot close two inches. Forward, not closed.

Through Grain 13–18 the harvest crosses under the roof in two physical steps and neither becomes an account. The first cut grain comes in off the strips and lies covered near the hearth; then, across Grain 16–18, it is taken out of its wool, beaten on the flagstones with an ash-wood flail, winnowed so the chaff goes up on the river wind, spread thin to finish drying and poured back under the fold --- dressed and stored, its arms reading weight and not cost, the yard counting it in glances and handfuls, no slate opened and no figure given. The boy takes it apart as he takes apart everything within two paces: what it is, where it stood, who paid a figure for it, what it asks of him; then files it on a place of its own, not across the shelf's two gaps, the fold's one crust and its empty place, the pan's gone ring or the field's empty place. With the dressing hands the yard goes around the returning-line place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T098 advanced and unpaid). His private file is turned out like instruments on a towel and is exact, complete and worth nothing: six hearings with no seventh, the seventh green a colour and seventh shape a shape on places that do not touch, nine stoppings with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, three held changes on three places with the loft scrape fourth, the pale line fifth and the held inch sixth. Across six scenes the hand answers six times and ends with the thirtieth answer: up an inch toward the river's note, carried through the small intake, the hold and the letting go of one of Sella's counted breaths in the room that does not touch his, kept through the silence after her count and brought down on the river's next low note --- two rhythms across a wall that does not touch, remembered, not a power, a reach, a hearing or a payment. He still cannot close the two inches to the pot handle, lift his chin to the crust or speak a believable number. Forward into Grain 19 and the second reading, not closed.

Through Grain 19–21 (Days 228–230) he reaches two hundred and thirty days — six months and fifty — with nobody saying what the number counts, and Grain's second Account-day changes the district's arithmetic where two hundred people stood while the figure read over his basket stays not the figure paid. He takes that machine apart as he takes a wound apart, reads the covered grain as a keeping and not a figure, and takes a cost asked as a direction to the pot and the weather; the surgeon's exactness remains complete and worth nothing at two paces. The file laid out in the small hours of Day 230 gains one new place: T101, the question about the row asked by a voice with no owner and answered with the weather and the price of barley, filed on a seventh place of its own with a finger between it and the pot sayings, the burned slate, the shut-room tellings, the hearings and the count — not a fourth shaping, not a hearing, not a seventh, not a cause, not a name, held for the far-off day a witness stands somewhere and can be asked. His hand answers six more times and the thirty-sixth carries an inch through one of Sella's counted breaths across a wall that does not touch, down on the river's next low note: two rhythms remembered, filed on the sixth place and not a power, a reach or a sign. He still cannot close two inches to the pot handle, lift his chin to the leathery crust or speak a believable number, and wants the crust, the handle and a number spoken by somebody who would have known what a number counted; none comes. Nothing is joined, named, opened, called, spent or ended, and the count goes forward unspent into Grain's last tend.

Through Grain 22–24 (Days 231–233) the last tend of the harvest month is lived as ordinary tending under the Vail roof, with no market, no Cross and no bell and Grain 30 six mornings off. He is two hundred and thirty-one, thirty-two and thirty-three days old — six months and fifty-one to fifty-three — and nobody says either figure. The house keeps its time in the kiln's four pulls and the silence where the fifth should stand, the river's note through the finger's-width door, and the boards' four nails, four and a thumb-width knot: three counts in three places that do not become one, for agreeing them would want a payer and none stands to any. The file is turned out as instruments on a towel and found exact, complete and worth nothing. In the afternoon of Day 232 the chapter's one movement comes: a voice with no owner asks over the boards which row the pot went quiet on, the weather and the price of barley answer after it, the voice goes off up the terrace and the row stands where it stood. He takes the question apart as a surgeon takes a wound apart and sets the second telling on the seventh place beside the first with a finger between the two, and fingers between it and the three pot sayings, the fourth burned-slate telling, the two shut-room tellings, the six hearings and the count. Two askings are not one witness; a witness stands somewhere and can be asked, and none does. It is not a fourth shaping, a hearing, a seventh of the full note, a fifth telling of the slate, a cause, a name or a fifth witness — a seed for the far-off day a witness stands somewhere and can be asked (T101 advanced, not paid). His hand answers six times across the three days, the thirty-seventh through the forty-second, the last a two-rhythm coordination held through one of Sella's counted breaths across the wall and down on the river's next low note, remembered and not a change made again. He still cannot close two inches to the pot handle, lift his chin to the leathery crust or speak a believable number. Nothing is joined, named, opened, called, spent or ended; the count goes forward exact and unspent into Day 234.

Through Grain 25–27 (Days 234–236) the last tend of the harvest month is lived as ordinary tending under the Vail roof, with no market, no Cross, no bell and no Account-day and Grain 30 three mornings off at the close. He is two hundred and thirty-four, thirty-five and thirty-six days old — six months and fifty-four to fifty-six — and nobody says either figure. The house keeps its time in the kiln's four pulls and the silence where the fifth should stand, the river's note through the finger's-width door, and the boards' four nails, four and a thumb-width knot: three counts in three places that do not become one, for agreeing them would want a payer and none stands to any. The file is turned out as instruments on a towel and found exact, complete and worth nothing. On the afternoon of Day 235 the ownerless question comes a third time: the weather answers first, the price of barley after it, the voice goes off up the terrace and the row stands where it stood. He takes it apart as a surgeon takes a wound apart — what it asks, where it stands, what it would want before any man could answer it and who would stand under the answer to pay — and finds no witness at the bottom; he sets the third telling on the seventh place beside the first two with a finger between each pair and fingers between them and the sayings, the tellings, the hearings and the count. Three askings are not one witness; a witness stands somewhere and can be asked, and none does. It is not a fourth shaping, a hearing, a seventh, a fifth telling, a cause, a name or a fifth witness — a seed for the far-off day a witness stands somewhere and can be asked (T101 advanced, not paid). His hand answers six times across the three days, the forty-third through the forty-eighth, the forty-eighth a two-rhythm coordination held across the wall through one of Sella's counted breaths, past the silence after her count and down on the river's next low note, remembered and not a change made again. He still cannot close two inches to the pot handle, lift his chin to the leathery crust or speak a believable number. Nothing is joined, named, opened, called, spent or ended; the count goes forward exact and unspent into Day 237.

## Chapter Log

- Ch 61: Lived Days 234–236, Grain 25–27, Fifthday to Seventhday, three days in six lived scenes, each day opened by its own `\daymark`, two hundred and thirty-four through two hundred and thirty-six days old — six months and fifty-four days to six months and fifty-six — nobody saying either figure under any roof (T071/T072). No market, no Cross, no bell and no Account-day; Grain 20 (Day 229) stood behind with its changed arithmetic unread under this roof and Grain 30 (Day 239) stood three mornings ahead at the close and was not brought early. First smell hot clay under tallow and wet wool under that, with the sour Ash breath of the unswept corner the house calls the thaw; first sound the kiln drawing in fours with a gap, not a bell. **The chapter's one movement, advanced and not paid:** in the afternoon of Day 235 a voice with no owner, no room, no hand at the kiln and no cause came over the boards again, low into the boards and not into the basket, asking which row the pot went quiet on; the weather answered first and the price of barley after it, and the voice went up the terrace while the row stood where it stood, with nobody leaning in to take the answer down. He took the question apart as a surgeon takes apart a wound — what it asked, where it stood, what it would want before any man could answer it and who would stand under the answer to pay — and found no witness at the bottom. He set the third telling on the seventh place beside the first two with a finger between each pair and a finger between them and the sayings, the tellings, the hearings and the count. Three askings are not one witness, for a witness stands somewhere and can be asked and none does and none could; not a fourth shaping, not a hearing, not a seventh of the full note, not a fifth telling of the slate, not a cause, not a name and not a fifth witness — a seed for the far-off day a witness stands somewhere and can be asked (T101 advanced, not paid). The file turned out on the towel was exact, complete and worth nothing: six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers, three shapings of the pot saying, the fourth burned-slate telling and the two shut-room tellings on their own places, told bell beside heard, told figure beside paid, nine stoppings with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, Seed 16 unspent, the loan uncalled, three held changes plus the loft scrape, the pale line and the held inch, the seventh green a colour and the seventh shape a shape with no eighth, the dressed grain covered and unfigured, the returning-line place gone around with a look and no number, and the question on its seventh place with three askings and nobody to own it. Six answers of the hand across the three days, the forty-third through the forty-eighth: down at once, held to the small intake, down before the counted breath, kept to the river's own low interval, a miss entered without shame, and in the small hours of Day 236 the forty-eighth taken through the small intake, the hold and the letting-go of one of Sella's breaths in the room that does not touch his, kept through the little silence after her count and brought down on the river's next low note — two rhythms held one against the other across a wall that does not touch, remembered, not a longer reach, miss entered, hearing, seventh, stopping, payment, held change, key, power or rule, filed on the sixth place with no payer named (T061/T082). The widow's mark hung untouched with no opening in fifty-four, fifty-five and fifty-six days; Sella gave the fire its instruction before the basket, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, said *The house will keep it*, sent cost-questions to the pot and the weather, shut her hand half over nothing and kept her finger on top of the shelf's front edge so the charcoal stayed unread. At the kindling Tarn told the wood *Six counted. The next I don't count* with the knife at the lip, brought the ear-sentence to the calling word as a remembered stopping, held nine with no tenth and kept the Seed 16 breath unspent, chest tight, Ash in the knuckle creases, the pale clip-ring at his left wrist and the warm stone cold in its loft pot. At the wheel-shed Jorra parted dust from slip, withheld the note known three turns ahead, kept the burned-slate word at four and the shut-room tellings at two, put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence as a remembered repetition, kept *he's not Vail* sealed and the promise ungiven. Garl stayed in objects with his name unspoken two hundred and thirty-six days and no collector on Grain 25, 26 or 27. Nothing joined, named, opened, called, spent or ended; the count goes forward exact and unspent into Day 237.

- Ch 60: Lived Days 231–233, Grain 22–24, Secondday to Fourthday, three days in six lived scenes, each day opened by its own `\daymark`, two hundred and thirty-one through two hundred and thirty-three days old — six months and fifty-one days to six months and fifty-three — nobody saying either figure under any roof (T071/T072). No market, no Cross, no bell and no Account-day; Grain 20 (Day 229) stood behind and Grain 30 (Day 239) six mornings ahead and was not brought early. First smell hot clay under tallow and wet wool under that, with the sour Ash breath of the unswept corner the house calls the thaw; first sound the kiln drawing in fours with a gap, not a bell. **The chapter's one movement, advanced and not paid:** in the afternoon of Day 232 a voice with no owner, no room, no hand at the kiln and no cause asked over the boards which row the pot went quiet on; the weather answered first and the price of barley after it, and the voice went up the terrace with the row standing where it stood. He took it apart as a surgeon takes apart a wound — what it asked, where it stood, what it would want before any man could answer it, and who would stand under the answer to pay for it — and set the second telling on the seventh place beside the first with a finger between the two, and fingers between it and the three shapings of the pot saying, the fourth burned-slate telling, the two shut-room tellings, the six hearings of the full note and the count. Two askings are not one witness, for a witness stands somewhere and can be asked and none did; not a fourth shaping, not a hearing, not a seventh of the full note, not a fifth telling of the slate, not a cause, not a name and not a fifth witness — a seed for the far-off day a witness stands somewhere and can be asked (T101 advanced, not paid). The file turned out on the towel was exact, complete and worth nothing: six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers, three pot sayings, the fourth burned-slate telling and the two shut-room tellings on their own places, told bell beside heard, told figure beside paid with Grain's second arithmetic changed and unread under the roof, nine stoppings with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, three held changes plus the loft scrape, the carried man's pale line and the held inch, the seventh green a colour and the seventh shape a shape with no eighth, the dressed grain covered and unfigured, the returning-line place gone around with no number, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled. The hand answered six times — thirty-seventh up an inch toward the river's note and down at once, thirty-eighth held to the small intake only, thirty-ninth down before the counted breath, fortieth kept to the river's own low interval with no count in the house to borrow, forty-first down before the counted breath had finished, and forty-second in the small hours of Day 233 carried through the small intake, the hold and the letting go of one of Sella's breaths in the room that does not touch his, kept through the little silence after her count had ended and brought down on the river's next low note — a two-rhythm coordination held across the wall, remembered, not a power, a reach, a hearing, a seventh, a stopping, a payment, a held change, a key or a rule, filed on the sixth place with no payer named. He still could not close two inches to the pot handle, lift the leathery crust two inches off or speak a believable number. Sella, Tarn and Jorra kept their old grammar; Garl stayed in objects; Berr, Hessa, the clerk, the green-mouthed woman and the foundling stayed offstage. Nothing joined, named, opened, called, spent or ended; the ledger held at 101 threads (66 planted, 34 open, 1 paid), no thread opened and none paid. Forward into Day 234, not closed.

- Ch 59: Lived Days 228–230, Grain 19–21, Ninthday to Firstday, three days in six lived scenes, each day opened by its own `\daymark`, two hundred and twenty-eight through two hundred and thirty days old — six months and forty-eight days to six months and fifty — nobody saying either figure under any roof (T071/T072). Grain 20 (Day 229) was the **second Account-day and market of Grain**; Grain 19 and 21 were ordinary tending days, and Grain 30 (Day 239) stood nine mornings ahead and was not brought early. First smell hot clay under tallow and wet wool under that, with the sour Ash breath of the unswept corner the house calls the thaw; first sound the kiln drawing in fours with a gap, not a bell. The carried fire gave four low pulls and withheld its fifth, its payer unnamed for 228, 229 and 230 days, while the river's note of water over water through the finger's-width door and the boards' four nails, four and a thumb-width knot kept three counts in three places that do not become one, for agreeing them would want a payer and none stands to any (T088, T061/T082). One stiff leathery brown-edged barley crust lay two inches from his ear with its empty place unnamed and no third; the thread at his mouth was frozen and unwiped; cold stopped at cheek, temple and the bare wrist-joint where the wool rode back, with the afterdrop waiting past a warming nobody in this house would perform. **The second Account-day, gathered and not paid:** in the square on Day 229 Sella set the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade where no slate lay and stood half a pace off; Berr came up short off the clay road with the red wool cord folded square, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, passed a finger over a narrow slate held unopened, read *one in twelve, as the notice says* low into the crowd with the figure going twice over the basket, called the levy and denied it with *your house*, never *I*, and went off up the road with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, believing he had done a kindness; Hessa carried the slate up unread and the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set below the north bridge for a measure nobody called; the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself. The district's arithmetic changed for the second time in Grain and cannot be changed back, while the printed figure stands unchallenged and a figure read aloud is still not the figure paid, and the reading answered nothing for the man carried out on Day 188 or the hand that went in on Seed 15 and did not come back. The dressed grain lay covered near the hearth with no figure over it, read over by a number that did not know what it cost (T015/T041/T047, T077, T078, T080/T090). **The chapter's one new thread, T101, planted and unpaid:** on Grain 21 a voice with no owner, room, hand at the kiln or cause asked over the boards which row the pot went quiet on; the weather answered and the price of barley after it, and the row stood where it stood. He took the question apart as a surgeon takes apart a wound and filed it on a **seventh place of its own** with a finger between it and the three pot sayings, the fourth burned-slate telling, the two shut-room tellings, the six hearings and the count — not a fourth shaping, not a hearing, not a seventh, not a cause, not a name and not a fifth telling of the slate, but a seed for the far-off day a witness stands somewhere and can be asked (T101 planted). T098 was advanced and emphatically not paid: the returning-line place went around with a look and no number on all three days. **The hand answered six times across six scenes**, the thirty-first down at once under conditions that had sometimes answered, the thirty-second held to the small intake and down before the letting-go, the thirty-third down before the counted breath at the market's edge, the thirty-fourth kept to the river's own low interval with no house-count to borrow, the thirty-fifth down at once under conditions that had once answered, and the thirty-sixth an inch carried through the small intake, the hold and the letting go of one of Sella's breaths in the room that does not touch his, kept through the little silence after her count and brought down on the river's next low note — two rhythms held across a wall, remembered, not a change made again, filed on the sixth place with no payer named and not a power, reach, hearing, seventh, stopping, payment, held change, key or rule. Thirty-six answers since Day 213. The file turned out in the small hours of Day 230 was exact, complete and worth nothing: six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers, three shapings of the pot saying, the fourth burned-slate telling, two shut-room tellings, told bell beside heard, told figure beside paid, nine stoppings with no tenth before the seventh year at its very end, three held changes plus the loft scrape, the pale line and the held inch, and the seed of T101 on its seventh place. Nothing joined, named, opened, called, spent or ended; forward into Day 231, Grain 22, Secondday. No new scar, possession or change of voice; the old life stays offstage.

- Ch 58: Lived Days 225–227, Grain 16–18, Sixthday to Eighthday, three days in six lived scenes, each day opened by its own `\daymark`, two hundred and twenty-five through two hundred and twenty-seven days old --- six months and forty-five days to six months and forty-seven --- nobody saying either figure under any roof (T071/T072). No market, no Cross and no bell through the middle tend; Grain 20 (Day 229) waited two mornings beyond the close and was not brought early. First smell hot clay under tallow and wet wool under that, with the sour Ash breath of the unswept corner the house calls the thaw; first sound the kiln drawing in fours with a gap, not a bell. The carried fire gave four low pulls and withheld its fifth, its payer unnamed for 225, 226 and 227 days, while the river kept water over water through the finger's-width door and the boards kept four nails, four and a thumb-width knot --- three counts in three places that do not become one, for agreeing them would want a payer and none stands to any (T088, T061/T082). One stiff brown-edged barley crust lay two inches from the ear with its empty place unnamed and no third; the thread at his mouth was frozen and unwiped; cold stopped at cheek, temple and the bare wrist-joint where the wool rode back with the afterdrop waiting past a warming nobody would perform. **The chapter's one meaningful change, gathered and not paid:** the year's first grain came out of its covering, was beaten out on the flagstones with an ash-wood flail and winnowed so the chaff went up on the river wind, then spread thin to finish drying and poured back under wool near the hearth --- dressed and stored, changed in state three times and given no figure, counted in glances and handfuls and by the carrying arm's weight, entered on no slate and laid across none of the absences (T077, T060/T086/T097). With the dressing hands the yard went around the returning-line place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number --- no name, cause, body, figure or payment; T098 advanced and emphatically not paid (T009/T013/T092). The file laid out as instruments on a towel was exact, complete and worth nothing: six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers, three shapings of the pot saying and the fourth burned-slate telling on their own places with the two shut-room tellings still two, told bell beside heard, told figure beside paid with Grain's first reading still not the figure paid and the true subtraction locked in Berr's box, nine stoppings with no tenth at the seventh year's very end, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled, the seventh green a colour and the seventh shape a shape on places that do not touch, with no eighth of either and neither named, joined or promoted (T061/T082, T050/T051, T033/T034 held).
